The new dog was very long, very brown, very friendly and charming. When it had had its supper it wagged its tail, turned a clear and gentle eye on Dickie, and without any warning stood on its head.

“Well,” said Mr. Beale, “if there ain’t money in that beast! A trick dog ’e is. ’E’s wuth wot I give for ’im, so ’e is. Knows more tricks than that ’ere, I’ll be bound.”

He did. He was a singularly well-educated dog. Next morning Mr. Beale, coming downstairs, was just in time to bang the front door in the face of Amelia coming in, pail-laden, from “doing” the steps, and this to prevent the flight of the new dog. The door of one of the dog-rooms was open, and a fringe of inquisitive dogs ornamented the passage.

===Chinese===
这条新狗身子很长，毛色深棕，又和气又惹人疼。吃过晚饭，它摇了摇尾巴，把一只清亮温和的眼睛转向迪基，然后毫无预兆地来了个倒立。

「瞧见没，」比尔先生说，「这畜生身上可藏着钱呢！它是条会耍把戏的狗。我给它的那几个钱，值，就是这么值。会的把戏还不止这一手，我敢打包票。」

它果然不止这一手。这是一条教养出奇好的狗。第二天早晨，比尔先生下楼时，恰好来得及把前门砰地带上，堵住了阿米莉亚的脸——她拎着水桶，「擦洗」完台阶正要进门——为的是拦住新狗逃跑。有一间狗屋的门敞开着，一长溜好奇的狗脑袋在走廊上排成了流苏似的饰边。

===Original===
“What you open that door at all for?” Mr. Beale asked Amelia.

“I didn’t,” she said, and stuck to it.

That afternoon Beale, smoking in the garden, got up, as he often did, to look through the window at the dogs. He gazed a moment, muttered something, and made one jump to the back door. It was closed. Amelia was giving the scullery floor a “thorough scrub over,” and had fastened the door to avoid having it opened with suddenness against her steaming pail or her crouching form.

===Chinese===
「那扇门你到底开它做什么？」比尔先生质问阿米莉亚。

「我没开，」她说，一口咬定到底。

那天下午，比尔在园子里抽烟，照老习惯站起身，隔着窗户看看狗。他凝神望了一会儿，嘟哝了一句，一个箭步蹿到后门口。门关着。阿米莉亚正在给洗涤间的地板来一次「彻底刷洗」，把门闩牢了，免得有人猛地推门，撞上她那冒着热气的水桶，或是她蹲着的身子。

===Original===
But Mr. Beale got in at the backdoor and out at the front just in time to see the dachshund disappearing at full speed, “like a bit of brown toffee-stick,” as he said, round the end of the street. They never saw that dog again.

“Trained to it,” Mr. Beale used to say sadly whenever he told the story; “trained to it from a pup, you may lay your life. I see ’im as plain as I see you. ’E listens an’ ’e looks, and ’e doesn’t ’ear nor see nobody. An’ ’e ups on his ’ind legs and turns the ’andle with ’is little twisty front pawses, clever as a monkey, and hout ’e goes like a harrow in a bow. Trained to it, ye see. I bet his master wot taught ’im that’s sold him time and again, makin’ a good figure every time, for ’e was a ’andsome dawg as ever I see. Trained the dawg to open the door and bunk ’ome. See? Clever, I call it.”

“It’s a mean trick,” said Dickie when Beale told him of the loss of the dog; “that’s what I call it. I’m sorry you’ve lost the dog.”

===Chinese===
可比尔先生从后门进去，打前门出来，赶到街上，恰好看见那条腊肠犬全速远去，「像一截棕色的太妃糖棍」，他这么说——绕过街尾不见了。那条狗他们从此再没见过。

「那是练出来的，」比尔先生每回讲起这件事，总是伤心地说；「打小狗起就练出来的，你拿性命打赌都行。我看得清清楚楚，就像这会儿看着你。它听听，看看，谁也不理不睬。然后后腿一立，用两只小前爪拧那个门把，灵得像猴子，跟着嗖——像离弦之箭一样蹿出去了。练出来的，你懂吧。我敢打赌，那教它这一手的主人，把它卖了一回又一回，每回都卖个好价钱，因为它是我见过顶标致的狗。把狗训练得会开门、好跑回家。明白吗？我说，这才叫机灵。」

「这把戏真损，」比尔把丢狗的经过告诉他时，迪基说；「我就是这么个叫法。你丢了狗，我替你难受。」

===Original===
“I ain’t exactly pleased myself,” said Beale, “but no use crying over broken glass. It’s the cleverness I think of most,” he said admiringly. “Now I’d never a thought of a thing like that myself﻿—not if I’d lived to a hundred, so I wouldn’t. *You* might ’ave,” he told Dickie flatteringly, “but I wouldn’t myself.”

“We don’t need to,” said Dickie hastily. “We earns our livings. We don’t need to cheat to get our livings.”

“No, no, dear boy,” said Mr. Beale, more hastily still; “course we don’t. That’s just what I’m a-saying, ain’t it? We shouldn’t never ’ave thought o’ that. No need to, as you say. The cleverness of it!”

===Chinese===
「我自己也说不上痛快，」比尔说，「可打碎了的杯子，哭也无益。叫我念念不忘的，是那份机灵，」他叹服地说。「那样的点子，我自己这辈子是想不出来的——活到一百岁也想不出来，真的想不出来。*你*兴许想得出来，」他奉承地对迪基说，「我自己可不行。」

「咱们用不着想那个，」迪基急忙说。「咱们凭本事挣饭吃。用不着靠骗人挣饭吃。」

「不，不，好孩子，」比尔先生抢得更急地说；「咱们当然用不着。我说的不就是这个意思，是不是？咱们压根儿不会往那上头想。用不着，照你说的。多机灵啊，那手绝活！」

===Original===
This admiration of the cleverness by which he himself had been cheated set Dickie thinking. He said, very gently and quietly, after a little pause﻿—

“This ’ere walking tower of ours. We pays our own way? No cadging?”

“I should ’ope you know me better than that,” said Beale virtuously; “not a patter have I done since I done the Rally and started in the dog line.”

===Chinese===
比尔这样叹服那份骗了他自己的机灵，倒叫迪基暗暗琢磨起来。歇了一小会儿，他轻轻的、静静的说——

「咱们这趟徒步远游。一路的花销都是自己掏的？没白吃白拿过人家的？」

「我想你总该更明白我的为人，」比尔一脸正气地说；「打从办完那次『集会』、改行做起狗生意起，讨饭的那一套我就一句没沾过。」

===Original===
“Nor yet no dealings with that redheaded chap what I never see?”

“Now, is it likely?” Beale asked reproachfully. “I should ’ope we’re a cut above a low chap like wot ’e is. The pram’s dry as a bone and shiny as yer ’at, and we’ll start the first thing in the morning.”

And in the early morning, which is fresh and sweet even in Deptford, they bade farewell to Amelia and the dogs and set out.

===Chinese===
「也没跟那个我从来没见过的红头发家伙有过什么瓜葛？」

「你说有这可能吗？」比尔带着受了委屈的神气反问。「我盼着咱们总比他那种下三滥高出那么一头。婴儿车干得透透的，亮得像你的帽子，明儿一早就动身。」

于是在大清早——即便在德普特福德，清晨也自有它的清新甜美——他们向阿米莉亚和狗群道了别，动身出发。

===Original===
Amelia watched them down the street and waved a farewell as they turned the corner. “It’ll be a bit lonesome,” she said. “One thing, I shan’t be burgled, with all them dogs in the house.”

The voices of the dogs, as she went in and shut the door, seemed to assure her that she would not even be so very lonely.

And now they were really on the road. And they were going to Arden﻿—to that place by the sea where Dickie’s uncle, in the other life, had a castle, and where Dickie was to meet his cousins, after his seven months of waiting.

===Chinese===
阿米莉亚目送他们走远，等他们转过街角，又挥手道了别。「往后要有点儿冷清喽，」她说。「好歹有一桩：满屋子养着这些狗，贼是不敢来光顾的。」

她进屋掩上门的时候，狗群的声音仿佛向她担保：她甚至连那么点儿孤单都不会有。

如今，他们是真真切切地上路了。他们这是上阿登（Arden）去——上海边那个地方去：在那另一种生活里，迪基的叔叔在那里有一座城堡；熬过了这七个月的等待，迪基就要在那里见到他的表亲了。

===Original===
You may think that Dickie would be very excited by the thought of meeting, in this workaday, nowadays world, the children with whom he had had such wonderful adventures in the other world, the dream world﻿—too excited, perhaps, to feel really interested in the little everyday happenings of “the road.” But this was not so. The present was after all the real thing. The dreams could wait. The knowledge that they were there, waiting, made all the ordinary things more beautiful and more interesting. The feel of the soft dust underfoot, the bright, dewy grass and clover by the wayside, the lessening of houses and the growing wideness of field and pasture, all contented and delighted Dickie. He felt to the full all the joy that Mr. Beale felt in “ ’oofing it,” and when as the sun was sinking they overtook a bent, slow-going figure, it was with a thrill of real pleasure that Dickie recognized the woman who had given him the blue ribbon for True.

True himself, now grown large and thick of coat, seemed to recognize a friend, gambolled round her dreadful boots, sniffed at her withered hand.

“Give her a lift with her basket, shall us?” Dickie whispered to Mr. Beale and climbed out of the perambulator. “I can make shift to do this last piece.”

===Chinese===
你也许会想，一想到就要在这个平凡的当今世界里，同他在另一个世界——梦的世界——里共过那许多奇遇的孩子们相会，迪基准会激动得不得了，说不定激动得对「路上」的种种日常小事都提不起真正的兴致来了。其实不然。眼前这一切，毕竟才是实实在在的东西。梦，尽可以等着。知道他们在那边等着，反倒叫一切平常事物变得更美、更有意思了。脚下松软的尘土，路畔沾着露水、亮闪闪的青草和三叶草，房屋渐渐稀少，田野和牧场的天地渐渐开阔——样样都叫迪基心满意足、满心欢喜。比尔先生在「迈开腿走」里的那一份快乐，他体会了个十足；所以当日头西沉、他们赶上前面一个佝偻着背、慢吞吞赶路的身影时，迪基认出了那个曾把蓝丝带送给特鲁（True）的妇人，一阵真真切切的欢喜掠过心头。

特鲁自己如今也长大了，毛也厚了，似乎也认出了这位朋友，绕着她那双吓人的大靴子撒欢，凑上去嗅她那只枯瘦的手。

「咱们帮她提提篮子吧，好不好？」迪基对着比尔先生耳语，爬出了婴儿车。「最后这一截路，我凑合着还能走。」

===Original===
So the three went on together, in friendly silence. As they neared Orpington the woman said, “Our road parts here; and thank you kindly. A kindness is never wasted, so they say.”

“That ain’t nothing,” said Beale; “besides, there’s the blue ribbon.”

“That the dog?” the woman asked.

===Chinese===
于是他们三个一同前行，和和气气地谁也不作声。快到奥平顿（Orpington）的时候，妇人说：「咱们的路到这儿就分手了；多谢你们的好心。人家说，好意是从来不会白费的。」

「这算不得什么，」比尔说；「再说，还有那条蓝丝带呢。」

「说的是这条狗？」妇人问。

===Original===
“Same ole dawg,” said Beale, with pride.

“A pretty beast,” she said. “Well﻿—so long.”

She looked back to smile and nod to them when she had taken her basket and the turning to the right, and Dickie suddenly stiffened all over, as a pointer does when it sees a partridge.

===Chinese===
「正是那条老狗，」比尔得意地说。

「一条挺俊的狗，」她说。「好了——回见。」

她提起了篮子，拐上朝右的岔路，回头又朝他们笑了笑、点了点头；迪基却猛地浑身一僵，活像指示猎犬瞧见了鹧鸪。

===Original===
“I say,” he cried, “you’re the nurse﻿—”

“I’ve nursed a many in my time,” she called back.

“But in the dream﻿ ﻿… you know.”

===Chinese===
「我说，」他喊道，「您就是那位保姆——」

「我这辈子看护过的人可多着呢，」她回头应道。

「可是在梦里……您知道的呀。」

===Original===
“Dreams is queer things,” said the woman. “And,” she added, “least said is soonest mended.”

“But﻿ ﻿…” said Dickie.

“Keep your eyes open and your mouth shut’s a good motto,” said she, nodded again, and turned resolutely away.

===Chinese===
「梦这东西，可怪着呢，」妇人说。「再说，」她添了一句，「话说得越少，事情越容易弥缝。」

「可是……」迪基说。

「眼睛放亮，嘴巴关紧，这是句好格言，」她说，又点了点头，断然转身去了。

===Original===
“Not very civil, I don’t think,” said Beale, “considerin’﻿—”

“Oh, she’s all right,” said Dickie, wondering very much, and very anxious that Beale should not wonder. “May I ride in the pram, farver? My foot’s a bit blistered, I think. We ain’t done so much walkin’ lately, ’ave us?”

“Ain’t tired in yourself, are you?” Mr. Beale asked, “ ’cause there’s a place called Chevering Park, pretty as a picture﻿—I thought we might lay out there. I’m a bit ’ot in the ’oof meself; but I can stick it if you can.”

Dickie could; and when they made their evening camp in a deep gully soft with beech-leaves, and he looked out over the ridge﻿—cautiously, because of keepers﻿—at the smoothness of a mighty slope, green-gray in the dusk, where rabbits frisked and played, he was glad that he had not yielded to his tiredness and stopped to rest the night anywhere else. Chevering Park is a very beautiful place, I would have you to know. And the travellers were lucky. The dogs were good and quiet, and no keeper disturbed their rest or their masters. Dickie slept with True in his arms, and it was like a draught of soft magic elixir to lie once more in the still, cool night and look up at the stars through the trees.

“Can’t think why they ever invented houses,” he said, and then he fell asleep.

===Chinese===
「依我说，这话说得可不算客气，」比尔说，「想想当初——」

「哦，她人挺好的，」迪基说；他心里稀罕得不得了，却又一心指望比尔别跟着稀罕。「爸爸，我能上车里坐坐吗？我脚上好像磨了个泡。咱们近来走得不多，是不是？」

「不是你自己累乏了吧？」比尔先生问，「因为有个叫切弗林庄园（Chevering Park）的地方，美得像画儿——我寻思咱们兴许能在那儿歇夜。我自己脚底下也有些发烫；可你顶得住，我就顶得住。」

迪基顶得住。傍晚扎营，他们在一道深沟里安顿下来，沟底铺满山毛榉落叶，软软的。迪基越过山脊往外望——望得小心翼翼，因为得提防猎场看守——望着那面大斜坡在暮色里青灰色的平缓身子，坡上兔子们蹦来跳去。他心里欢喜：幸亏没向疲倦低头，没在随便哪个别处停下来过夜。切弗林庄园是个非常美丽的地方，这你们可得知道。两位旅人也算运气好。狗儿们又乖又安静，没有任何看守来搅扰它们的清梦或它们的主人。迪基搂着特鲁睡；再一次躺在这宁静清凉的夜里，从树枝间仰望星星，就像饮下一剂柔柔的魔法琼浆。

「真想不明白，人当初干嘛要发明房子，」他说完便睡着了。

===Original===
By short stages, enjoying every step of every day’s journey, they went slowly and at their ease through the garden-land of Kent. Dickie loved every minute of it, every leaf in the hedge, every blade of grass by the roadside. And most of all he loved the quiet nights when he fell asleep under the stars with True in his arms.

It was all good, all﻿ ﻿… And it was worth waiting and working for seven long months, to feel the thrill that Dickie felt when Beale, as they topped a ridge of the great South Downs, said suddenly, “There’s the sea,” and, a dozen yards further on, “There’s Arden Castle.”

There it lay, gray and green, with its old stones and ivy﻿—the same Castle which Dickie had seen on the day when they lay among the furze bushes and waited to burgle Talbot Court. There were red roofs at one side of the Castle where a house had been built among the ruins. As they drew nearer, and looked down at Arden Castle, Dickie saw two little figures in its green courtyard, and wondered whether they could possibly be Edred and Elfrida, the little cousins whom he had met in King James the First’s time, and who, the nurse said, really belonged to the times of King Edward the Seventh, or Nowadays, just as he did himself. It seemed as though it could hardly be true; but, if it were true, how splendid! What games he and they could have! And what a play-place it was that spread out before him﻿—green and glorious, with the sea on one side and the downs on the other, and in the middle the ruins of Arden Castle.

===Chinese===
一程又一程，每天的每一步都细细受用，他们悠悠闲闲、慢慢腾腾地穿过肯特（Kent）的园圃之乡。迪基爱这里的每一分钟，爱树篱上的每一片叶子，爱路边的每一茎草。而他最爱的是那些安静的夜晚——搂着特鲁，在星空底下睡去。

这一切都好，一切都……为了体会迪基那一刻的激动——当他们翻上南唐斯（the South Downs）的一道山脊，比尔忽然说：「瞧，海。」再走上十几码，又说：「阿登城堡（Arden Castle）到了。」——为这个，苦苦等上七个月、操劳七个月，也是值的。

城堡就在那儿，灰绿相间，古石斑驳，常春藤蔓——正是迪基见过的那座城堡：那一天，他们躺在荆豆丛里，等着去盗塔尔博特府（Talbot Court）。城堡的一侧有几片红屋顶，那是有人在废墟中间盖起的一所房子。走得再近些，俯望阿登城堡的时候，迪基望见绿茵茵的院子里有两个小小的身影，心里直嘀咕：那该不会就是埃德雷德（Edred）和埃尔弗里达（Elfrida）吧——他在詹姆士一世的时代结识的两位小表亲；照保姆的说法，他们其实属于爱德华七世的时代，也就是「如今」的时代，跟他自己一样。这看起来简直不像是真的；可倘若是真的，那该有多好！他和他们能玩出多少游戏来！而他眼前铺开的这块游戏场地又是何等气派——青绿满目，辉煌壮丽，一边是海，一边是丘陵，正当中就是阿登城堡的废墟。

===Original===
But as they went on through the furze bushes Dickie perceived that Mr. Beale was growing more and more silent and uneasy.

“What’s up?” Dickie asked at last. “Out with it, farver.”

“It ain’t nothing,” said Mr. Beale.

===Chinese===
可当他们继续穿行在荆豆丛中的时候，迪基察觉比尔先生越来越沉默，也越来越不安。

「怎么啦？」迪基终于问道。「有话就说出来吧，爸爸。」

「没什么，」比尔先生说。

===Original===
“You ain’t afraid those Talbots will know you again?”

“Not much I ain’t. They never see my face; and I ’adn’t a beard that time like what I’ve got now.”

“Well, then?” said Dickie.

===Chinese===
「你不是怕塔尔博特家的人再认出你来吧？」

「才不呢。他们压根儿没瞧见过我的脸；再说那会儿我还没留上现在这把胡子。」

「那到底为什么？」迪基说。

===Original===
“Well, if you must ’ave it,” said Beale, “we’re a-gettin’ very near my ole dad’s place, and I can’t make me mind up.”

“I thought we was settled we’d go to see ’im.”

“I dunno. If ’e’s under the daisies I shan’t like it﻿—I tell you straight I shan’t like it. But we’re a long-lived stock﻿—p’raps ’e’s all right. I dunno.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，既然你非问不可，」比尔说，「咱们离我老爸的地方不远了，我心里拿不定主意。」

「我当咱们说定了要去看他的。」

「我不知道。要是他已经躺到雏菊花底下去了，我心里不会痛快——我跟你说实话，不会痛快。不过咱们家的人寿长——兴许他好端端的。我不知道。」

===Original===
“Shall I go up by myself to where he lives and see if he’s all right?”

“Not much,” said Mr. Beale; “if I goes I goes, and if I stays away I stays away. It’s just the not being able to make me mind up.”

“If he’s there,” said Dickie, “don’t you think you *ought* to go, just on the chance of him being there and wanting you?”

===Chinese===
「要不我自己一个人上他住的地方去，看看他好不好？」

「那可使不得，」比尔先生说；「要我去，我就去；不去，我就不去。难就难在拿不定这个主意。」

「他要是还在，」迪基说，「你不觉得你*该*去一趟吗？就冲着万一他还在、还惦记你，也该去。」

===Original===
“If you come to oughts,” said Beale, “I oughter gone ’ome any time this twenty year. Only I ain’t. See?”

“Well,” said Dickie, “it’s your lookout. I know what I should do if it was me.”

Remembrance showed him the father who had leaned on his shoulder as they walked about the winding walks of the pleasant garden in old Deptford﻿—the father who had given him the little horse, and insisted that his twenty gold pieces should be spent as he chose.

===Chinese===
「要说到该不该，」比尔说，「这二十年来，随便哪一年我都该回家。可我就是没回。明白吗？」

「那么，」迪基说，「这是你自己的事，你自己掂量。换作是我，我知道我该怎么办。」

记忆让他重又见到那位父亲：在老德普特福德那座怡人花园的曲折小径上散步时，那位父亲曾倚着他的肩膀——那位送给他一匹小马、又坚持要他把那二十枚金币随自己的心意花掉的父亲。

===Original===
“I dunno,” said Beale. “What you think? Eh, matey?”

“I think *let’s*,” said Dickie. “I lay if he’s alive it ’ud be as good as three Sundays in the week to him to see you. You was his little boy once, wasn’t you?”

“Ay,” said Beale; “he was wagoner’s mate to one of Lord Arden’s men. ’E used to ride me on the big carthorses. ’E was a fine setup chap.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道，」比尔说。「你是怎么想的？啊，老伙计？」

「我的意思：*去吧*，」迪基说。「我敢打赌，他要是活着，见了你准像一礼拜里过三个礼拜天那么乐。你从前是他的小儿子，不是吗？」

「是啊，」比尔说；「他给阿登勋爵（Lord Arden）手下的一个人当赶车帮手。他常让我骑在高头大拉车上。他当年是个又体面又精神的汉子。」

===Original===
To hear the name of Arden on Beale’s lips gave Dickie a very odd, half-pleasant, half-frightened feeling. It seemed to bring certain things very near.

“Let’s,” he said again.

“All right,” said Beale, “only if it all goes wrong it ain’t my fault﻿—an’ there used to be a footpath a bit further on. You cut through the copse and cater across the eleven-acre medder, and bear along to the left by the hedge an’ it brings you out under Arden Knoll, where my old man’s place is.”

===Chinese===
从比尔嘴里听到「阿登」这个姓氏，迪基心头涌起一种说不出的感觉，一半欢喜，一半害怕。它仿佛把某些东西一下子拉到了眼前。

「去吧，」他又说了一遍。

「好吧，」比尔说，「只是万一事情全砸了，可不能怪我——再往前一点儿，从前有条小路。你们穿过小树林，斜斜地穿过那块十一英亩的草甸，靠着树篱一直靠左走，就到了阿登圆丘（Arden Knoll）脚下，我老爹住的地方就在那儿。」

===Original===
So they cut and catered and bore along, and came out under Arden Knoll, and there was a cottage, with a very neat garden full of gay flowers, and a brick pathway leading from the wooden gate to the front door. And by the front door sat an old man in a Windsor chair, with a brown spaniel at his feet and a bird in a wicker cage above his head, and he was nodding, for it was a hot day, and he was an old man and tired.

“Swelp me, I can’t do it!” whispered Beale. “I’ll walk on a bit. You just arst for a drink, and sort of see ’ow the land lays. It might turn ’im up seeing me so sudden. Good old dad!”

He walked quickly on, and Dickie was left standing by the gate. Then the brown spaniel became aware of True, and barked, and the old man said, “Down, Trusty!” in his sleep, and then woke up.

===Chinese===
于是他们穿过树丛，斜过草甸，靠着树篱走，最后来到阿登圆丘脚下。那里有一座村舍，园子收拾得整整齐齐，开满了鲜艳的花儿，一条砖铺的小径从木栅门一直通到前门。前门边坐着一位老人，坐在温莎椅上，脚边卧着一只棕色猎犬，头顶上悬着一只柳条鸟笼，笼里有只鸟。老人正在打盹，因为天热，而他上了年纪，乏了。

「老天爷作证，我可迈不出这一步！」比尔低声说。「我先往前遛遛。你就上前讨口水喝，顺便探探风色。猛不丁地看见我，说不定他会不痛快。好爸爸！」

他快步走远了，迪基一个人留在栅门边站着。这时，那只棕色猎犬发觉了特鲁，汪汪叫起来，老人在睡梦里说了句：「趴下，特拉斯蒂（Trusty）！」接着便醒了。

===Original===
His clear old eyes set in many wrinkles turned full on Dickie by the gate.

“May I have a drink of water?” Dickie asked.

“Come in,” said the old man.

===Chinese===
他那双老而清澈的眼睛，嵌在层层皱纹中间，直直地对准了栅门边的迪基。

「我能讨口水喝吗？」迪基问。

「进来吧，」老人说。

===Original===
And Dickie lifted the latch of smooth, brown, sun-warmed iron, and went up the brick path, as the old man slowly turned himself about in the chair.

“Yonder’s the well,” he said; “draw up a bucket, if thy leg’ll let thee, poor little chap!”

“I draws water with my arms, not my legs,” said Dickie cheerfully.

===Chinese===
迪基掀开那光滑的褐色铁门闩——铁让太阳晒得温热——走上砖铺小径；老人在椅子里慢慢地转过身来。

「井在那头，」他说；「你打一桶上来吧，要是你的腿肯答应，可怜的小家伙！」

「我提水用的是胳膊，不是腿，」迪基快快活活地说。

===Original===
“There’s a blue mug in the washhouse window-ledge,” said the old man. “Fetch me a drop when you’ve had your drink, my lad.”

Of course, Dickie’s manners were too good for him to drink first. He drew up the dripping oaken bucket from the cool darkness of the well, fetched the mug, and offered it brimming to the old man. Then he drank, and looked at the garden ablaze with flowers﻿—blush-roses and damask roses, and sweet-williams and candytuft, white lilies and yellow lilies, pansies, larkspur, poppies, bergamot, and sage.

It was just like a play at the Greenwich Theatre, Dickie thought. He had seen a scene just like that, where the old man sat in the sun and the Prodigal returned.

===Chinese===
「洗衣房的窗台上有只蓝杯子，」老人说。「你喝完了，也给我捎一口来，小伙子。」

当然，迪基的礼数好得很，断不肯先喝。他从井里清凉的暗处把那只滴滴答答的橡木桶绞上来，取来杯子，满满地捧给老人。然后自己才喝，一边打量那座开满鲜花的园子——胭脂玫瑰和大马士革玫瑰，须苞石竹和屈曲花，白百合和黄百合，三色堇、飞燕草、罂粟、麝香薄荷和鼠尾草。

迪基心想，这简直就像格林尼治剧院里的一出戏。他看过一模一样的一幕：老人坐在太阳底下，浪子回来了。

===Original===
Dickie would not have been surprised to see Beale run up the brick path and throw himself on his knees, exclaiming, “Father, it is I﻿—your erring but repentant son! Can you forgive me? If a lifetime of repentance can atone﻿ ﻿…” and so on.

If Dickie had been Beale he would certainly have made the speech, beginning, “Father, it is I.” But as he was only Dickie, he said﻿—

“Your name’s Beale, ain’t it?”

===Chinese===
倘若比尔奔上砖径，扑通跪倒，喊道：「父亲，是我——你那误入歧途、如今悔悟的儿子！你能饶恕我吗？要是一辈子的悔悟能够赎罪的话……」如此等等，迪基也一点儿不会觉得意外。

假如迪基就是比尔，他准会把这篇开场白说出来，起头便是「父亲，是我」。可他只是迪基，于是他说——

「您是姓比尔吧？」

===Original===
“It might be,” old Beale allowed.

“I seen your son in London. ’E told me about yer garden.”

“I should a thought ’e’d a-forgot the garden same as ’e’s forgot me,” said the old man.

===Chinese===
「兴许是吧，」老比尔含含糊糊地认了。

「我在伦敦见过您儿子。他跟我念叨过您的花园。」

「我倒当他把花园忘啦，就像他把我忘了一样，」老人说。

===Original===
“ ’E ain’t forgot you, not ’e,” said Dickie; “ ’e’s come to see you, an ’e’s waiting outside now to know if you’d like to see ’im.”

“Then ’e oughter know better,” said the old man, and shouted in a thin, high voice, “Jim, Jim, come along in this minute!”

Even then Beale didn’t act a bit like the prodigal in the play. He just unlatched the gate without looking at it﻿—his hand had not forgotten the way of it, for all it was so long since he had passed through that gate. And he walked slowly and heavily up the path and said, “Hullo, dad!﻿—how goes it?”

===Chinese===
「他没忘您，他才没有呢，」迪基说；「他瞧您来了，这会儿就在外头候着，想问您愿不愿意见他。」

「那他就该懂事点儿，」老人说着，拿又尖又细的嗓子喊道：「吉姆，吉姆，快给我进来！」

这时候，比尔的举止还是一点儿不像戏里的浪子。他看都不看，随手就拨开了门闩——他的手没有忘掉这道门的路数，虽然打上回走过这道门，已经隔了这么多年。他慢慢地、沉沉地走上小径，说：「喂，爸爸！——近来可好？」

===Original===
And the old man looked at him with his eyes half shut and said, “Why, it *is* James﻿—so it is,” as if he had expected it to be someone quite different.

And they shook hands, and then Beale said, “The garden’s looking well.”

And the old man owned that the garden ’ud do all right if it wasn’t for the snails.

===Chinese===
老人半眯着眼睛望他，说：「咦，*真是*詹姆士——真是他，」倒好像他原本料定来的是另外什么人。

爷儿俩握了手，然后比尔说：「花园收拾得挺好。」

老人承认，要不是有蜗牛，花园本可以弄得不赖。

===Original===
That was all Dickie heard, for he thought it polite to go away. Of course, they could not be really affectionate with a stranger about. So he shouted from the gate something about “back presently,” and went off along the cart track towards Arden Castle and looked at it quite closely. It was the most beautiful and interesting thing he had ever seen. But he did not see the children.

When he went back the old man was cooking steak over the kitchen fire, and Beale was at the sink straining summer cabbage in a colander, as though he had lived there all his life and never anywhere else. He was in his shirtsleeves too, and his coat and hat hung behind the backdoor.

So then they had dinner, when the old man had set down the frying-pan expressly to shake hands with Dickie, saying, “So this is the lad you told me about. Yes, yes.” It was a very nice dinner, with cold gooseberry pastry as well as the steak and vegetables. The kitchen was pleasant and cozy though rather dark, on account of the white climbing rose that grew round the window. After dinner the men sat in the sun and smoked, and Dickie occupied himself in teaching the spaniel and True that neither of them was a dog who deserved to be growled at. Dickie had just thrown back his head in a laugh at True’s sulky face and stiffly planted paws, when he felt the old man’s dry, wrinkled hand under his chin.

===Chinese===
迪基听到的就这么多；他觉得识相地走开才算有礼。当然，有外人在场，爷儿俩没法真心真意地亲热。于是他在栅门边喊了一声，说是「回头就来」，便顺着车辙道朝阿登城堡走去，把城堡仔仔细细看了一回。这是他有生以来见过的最美、最有趣的东西。可他没有见到那两个孩子。

他回去的时候，老人正对着灶火煎牛排，比尔在水槽边，用漏勺滤着夏天的卷心菜，仿佛他一辈子都住在这里，从没在别处待过。他也只穿着衬衫，外套和帽子挂在后门后头。

老人特意放下煎锅，郑重其事地跟迪基握了手，说：「这就是你跟我说起的那个孩子。是啊，是啊。」然后他们才吃晚饭。晚饭好得很，除了牛排和蔬菜，还有凉的醋栗馅酥饼。厨房里舒适而暖和，只是有些暗，因为绕着窗户长了一丛白色的攀缘蔷薇。饭后，两个大人坐在太阳底下抽烟；迪基自得其乐，教那只猎犬和特鲁明白：它们俩谁也不是该挨咆哮的狗。迪基刚为着特鲁那副蔫头耷脑的臭脸、那四只直挺挺戳在地上的爪子仰头大笑，忽然觉得老人那只干枯多皱的手托到了自己下巴底下。

===Original===
“Let’s ’ave a look at you,” he said, and peered closely at the child. “Where’d you get that face, eh? What did you say your name was?”

“Harding’s his name,” said Beale. “Dickie Harding.”

“Dickie *Arden*, I should a-said if you’d asked *me*,” said the old man. “Seems to me it’s a reg’lar Arden face he’s got. But my eyes ain’t so good as wot they was. What d’you say to stopping along of me a bit, my boy? There’s room in the cottage for all five of us. My son James here tells me you’ve been’s good as a son to him.”

===Chinese===
「让我好好瞧瞧你，」他说，凑近了端详这孩子。「你这张脸是打哪儿来的，啊？方才你说你叫什么来着？」

「他姓哈丁，」比尔说。「迪基·哈丁（Dickie Harding）。」

「你若问*我*，我就要说他是迪基·*阿登*（Arden），」老人说。「依我看，他那张脸是地道的阿登家脸孔。不过我的眼睛可不及从前了。我的孩子，你愿不愿意留下来陪我住些日子？这村舍住得下咱们五口人。我儿子詹姆士方才告诉我，你待他，好得跟亲儿子一个样。」

===Original===
“I’d love it,” said Dickie. So that was settled. There were two bedrooms for Beale and his father, and Dickie slept in a narrow, whitewashed slip of a room that had once been a larder. The brown spaniel and True slept on the rag hearthrug in the kitchen. And everything was as cozy as cozy could be.

“We can send for any of the dawgs any minute if we feel we can’t stick it without ’em,” said Beale, smoking his pipe in the front garden.

“You mean to stay a long time, then,” said Dickie.

“I dunno. You see, I was born and bred ’ere. The air tastes good, don’t it? An’ the water’s good. Didn’t you notice the tea tasted quite different from what it does anywhere else? That’s the soft water, that is. An’ the old chap﻿ ﻿… Yes﻿—and there’s one or two other things﻿—yes﻿—I reckon us’ll stop on ’ere a bit.”

And Dickie was very glad. For now he was near Arden Castle, and could see it any time that he chose to walk a couple of hundred yards and look down. And presently he would see Edred and Elfrida. Would they know him? That was the question. Would they remember that he and they had been cousins and friends when James the First was King?

===Chinese===
「我乐意极了，」迪基说。事情就这么定下了。两间卧房给比尔和他父亲；迪基睡一间窄窄的、刷得雪白的小屋，那里从前是间储藏吃食的屋子。棕色猎犬和特鲁睡在厨房的碎布炉前毯上。样样都舒坦得不能再舒坦。

「要是觉得离了它们熬不住，咱们随时可以打发人去把狗接来，」比尔在前园里抽着烟斗说。

「这么说，你打算长住喽？」迪基说。

「说不上来。你知道，我生在这儿，长在这儿。这儿的空气闻着舒服，是不是？水也好。你没觉出这儿的茶跟别处的味道全然两样吗？就因为水软，是软水。还有老爷子……是啊——另外还有一两样……是啊——我看咱们就在这儿多住些日子。」

迪基心里欢喜极了。因为如今他离阿登城堡近了，只要他肯走个两三百码、登高一望，随时都能见到它。而不久之后，他就要见到埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达了。他们会认得他吗？问题就在这里。他们还记不记得，詹姆士一世在位的时候，他和他们曾是表亲，是朋友？

## IX Kidnapped / 第九章 被拐

===Original===
And now New Cross seemed to go backwards and very far away, its dirty streets, its sordid shifts, its crowds of anxious, unhappy people, who never had quite enough of anything, and Dickie’s home was in a pleasant cottage from whose windows you could see great green rolling downs, and the smooth silver and blue of the sea, and from whose door you stepped, not on to filthy pavements, but on to a neat brick path, leading between beds glowing with flowers.

Also, he was near Arden, the goal of seven months’ effort. Now he would see Edred and Elfrida again, and help them to find the hidden treasure, as he had once helped them to find their father.

This joyful thought put the crown on his happiness.
===Chinese===
此刻，纽克罗斯（New Cross）仿佛直往后退，退得远远的——退走了它那些肮脏的街道，退走了那些寒碜的、凑凑合合的营生，退走了那成群成群总是愁眉苦脸、样样都缺那么一点儿的人；而迪基（Dickie）的家，成了一座怡人的村舍，从窗口望出去，是绵延起伏的青青丘陵，是大海那平滑的银蓝水色；从门口跨出去，脚下的不是龌龊的人行道，而是一条齐齐整整的砖铺小径，两旁的花坛开得一片灿烂。

而且，他离阿登（Arden）近了——那是他七个月来辛苦努力的目标。如今他又能见到埃德雷德（Edred）和埃尔弗里达（Elfrida），帮他们找到那批藏起来的宝藏，就像他从前帮他们找回父亲一样。

这快乐的念头，给他的幸福又添了一顶王冠。

===Original===
But he presently perceived that though he was so close to Arden Castle he did not seem to be much nearer to the Arden children. It is not an easy thing to walk into the courtyard of a ruined castle and ring the bell of a strange house and ask for people whom you have only met in dreams, or as good as dreams. And I don’t know how Dickie would have managed if Destiny had not kindly come to his help, and arranged that, turning a corner in the lane which leads to the village, he should come face to face with Edred and Elfrida Arden. And they looked exactly like the Edred and Elfrida whom he had played with and quarrelled with in the dream. He halted, leaning on his crutch, for them to come up and speak to him. They came on, looking hard at him﻿—the severe might have called it staring﻿—looked, came up to him, and passed by without a word! But he saw them talking eagerly to each other.

Dickie was left in the lane looking after them. It was a miserable moment. But quite quickly he roused himself. They were talking to each other eagerly, and once Elfrida half looked round. Perhaps it was his shabby clothes that made them not so sure whether he was the Dickie they had known. If they did not know him it should not be his fault. He balanced himself on one foot, beat with his crutch on the ground, and shouted, “Hi!” and “Hullo!” as loud as he could. The other children turned, hesitated, and came back.

“What is it?” the little girl called out; “have you hurt yourself?” And she came up to him and looked at him with kind eyes.
===Chinese===
可他很快发觉，自己虽然离阿登城堡（Arden Castle）这样近，却好像并没有离阿登家的孩子们更近一点。走进一座废城堡的院子，去按一所陌生人家的门铃，求见你只在梦里——或者跟在梦里差不多——见过的人，这可不是件容易事。若不是命运好心来帮忙，安排他在通往村子的小巷里一拐弯，就跟埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达·阿登迎面撞上，我真不知道迪基该如何是好。他们俩的模样，同他在梦里一起玩耍、一起拌嘴的那两个埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达一模一样。他拄着拐杖站住，等他们上来搭话。他们一路走来，直勾勾地盯着他瞧——讲究点儿的人该管这叫瞪眼了——瞧着，走到跟前，却一言不发地走了过去！不过他看见他们俩正起劲地互相说着什么。

巷子里剩下迪基一个人，望着他们的背影。这一刻真不好受。可他很快便振作起来。他们俩还在起劲地交谈，埃尔弗里达有一回还回过头来望了半眼。也许是他这一身寒碜衣裳，叫他们拿不准他究竟是不是他们认识的那个迪基。就算他们认不出他，也不能算他的错。他用一只脚撑住身子，拿拐杖笃笃地敲地，扯开嗓子拼命喊：「喂！」「哈罗！」那两个孩子转过身，迟疑了一下，走了回来。

「怎么回事？」小姑娘大声问；「你摔着自己了？」她走到他跟前，用和善的眼睛端详他。

===Original===
“No,” said Dickie; “but I wanted to ask you something.”

The other two looked at him and at each other, and the boy said, “Righto.”

“You’re from the Castle, aren’t you?” he said. “I was wondering whether you’d let me go down and have a look at it?”
===Chinese===
「没有，」迪基说；「不过我想问你们一件事。」

另外两个打量打量他，又互相望望，男孩说：「成啊。」

「你们是从城堡来的，对吧？」他说。「我在想，你们肯不肯让我下去看看它？」

===Original===
“Of course,” said the girl. “Come on.”

“Wait a minute,” said Dickie, nerving himself to the test. If they didn’t remember him they’d think he was mad, and never show him the Castle. Never mind! Now for it!

“Did you ever have a tutor called Mr. Parados?” he asked. And again the others looked at him and at each other. “Parrot-nose for short,” Dickie hastened to add; “and did you ever shovel snow on to his head and then ride away in a carriage drawn by swans?”
===Chinese===
「当然肯，」姑娘说。「走吧。」

「等一等，」迪基说，鼓足勇气准备经受这场考验。他们要是想不起他，就会当他疯了，再也不会领他去看城堡。不管了！就来啦！

「你们从前有没有过一位家庭教师，叫帕拉多斯先生（Mr. Parados）的？」他问。那两个又打量打量他，又互相望望。「简称鹦鹉鼻（Parrot-nose），」迪基急忙补上一句；「你们有没有把雪铲到他的脑袋上，然后坐上天鹅拉的马车跑掉？」

===Original===
“It *is* you!” cried Elfrida, and hugged him. “Edred, it *is* Dickie! We were saying, *could* it be you? Oh! Dickie darling, how did you hurt your foot?”

Dickie flushed. “My foot’s always been like that,” he said, “in Nowadays time. When we met in the magic times I was like everybody else, wasn’t I?”

Elfrida hugged him again, and said no more about the foot. Instead, she said, “Oh, how ripping it is to really and truly find you here! We thought you couldn’t be real because we wrote a letter to you at the address it said on that bill you gave us. And the letter came back with ‘not known’ outside.”
===Chinese===
「*真是*你！」埃尔弗里达叫起来，一把抱住他。「埃德雷德，*真是*迪基！我们还在说呢，*会不会*真是你？哦！迪基，亲爱的，你的脚是怎么弄伤的？」

迪基脸红了。「我的脚一向就是这样的，」他说，「在当今（Nowadays）的时代里。咱们在魔法的岁月里见面的时候，我跟别人一样，不是吗？」

埃尔弗里达又抱了抱他，脚的事就再没提。她改口说：「哦，真的真的在这儿找到了你，多叫人高兴啊！我们还当你不是真的呢，因为我们照你给我们的那张招贴上印的地址给你写了信，信却给退了回来，信皮上批着『查无此人』。」

===Original===
“What address was it?” Dickie asked.

“Laurie Grove, New Cross,” Edred told him.

“Oh, that was just an address Mr. Beale made up to look grand with,” said Dickie. “I remember his telling me about it. He’s the man I live with; I call him father because he’s been kind to me. But my own daddy’s dead.”
===Chinese===
「那地址写的是什么？」迪基问。

「劳里格罗夫（Laurie Grove），纽克罗斯，」埃德雷德告诉他。

「哦，那不过是比尔先生（Mr. Beale）编出来撑门面的一个地址，」迪基说。「我记得他跟我讲过这件事。他就是跟我一块儿住的人；我管他叫爸爸，因为他待我好。可我自己的爸爸已经去世了。」

===Original===
“Let’s go up on the downs,” said Elfrida, “and sit down, and you tell us all about everything from the very beginning.”

So they went up and sat among the furze bushes, and Dickie told them all his story﻿—just as much of it as I have told to you. And it took a long time. And then they reminded each other how they had met in the magic or dream world, and how Dickie had helped them to save their father﻿—which he did do, only I have not had time to tell you about it; but it is all written in *The House of Arden*.
===Chinese===
「咱们上丘陵草地上去吧，」埃尔弗里达说，「坐下来，你把事事从头到尾都讲给我们听。」

于是他们上了坡，在金雀花丛中坐下，迪基把自己的身世从头讲了一遍——讲的部分，恰是我已经讲给你们听的那些。这花了很长时间。随后他们又互相提醒起当初在魔法世界、也就是梦的世界里相识的经过，还有迪基怎样帮他们救回了父亲——这确实是真的，只是我一直没有工夫讲给你们听；不过这一切全都写在《阿登之家》（The House of Arden）里了。

===Original===
“But our magic is all over now,” said Edred sadly. “We had to give up ever having any more magic, so as to get father back. And now we shall never find the treasure or be able to buy back the old lands and restore the Castle and bring the water back to the moat, and build nice, dry, warm, cozy cottages for the tenants. But we’ve got father.”

“Well, but look here,” said Dickie. “We got *my* magic all right, and old nurse said I could work it for you, and that’s really what I’ve come for, so that we can look for the treasure together.”

“That’s awfully jolly of you,” said Elfrida.
===Chinese===
「可我们的魔法如今全完了，」埃德雷德难过地说。「为了换回爸爸，我们只好从此不再有魔法。这样一来，我们就永远找不到宝藏了，也买不回旧日的田产，修不好城堡，护城河里也再引不回水，更不能给佃户们盖起漂亮、干爽、暖和、舒舒服服的小屋了。不过我们到底把爸爸换回来了。」

「哎，可你听我说呀，」迪基说。「*我的*魔法好好儿的，老保姆说过，我可以替你们行它，我这一趟来其实就为的这个，好让咱们一块儿去找宝藏。」

「你真是太够朋友了，」埃尔弗里达说。

===Original===
“What is your magic?” Edred asked; and Dickie pulled out Tinkler and the white seal and the moonseeds, and laid them on the turf and explained.

And in the middle of the explanation a shadow fell on the children and the Tinkler and the moonseeds and the seal, and there was a big, handsome gentleman looking down at them and saying﻿—

“Introduce your friend, Edred.”
===Chinese===
「你的魔法是什么？」埃德雷德问。迪基掏出叮铃（Tinkler）、白图章和月籽（moonseeds），摆在草皮上，讲给他们听。

讲解讲到一半，一片影子罩住了孩子们、叮铃、月籽和白图章，一位高大英俊的绅士正低头望着他们，说道——

「介绍一下你的朋友，埃德雷德。」

===Original===
“Oh, Dickie, this is my father,” cried Edred, scrambling up. And Dickie added very quickly, “My name’s Dick Harding.” It took longer for Dickie to get up because of the crutch, and Lord Arden reached his hand down to help him. He must have been a little surprised when the crippled child in the shabby clothes stood up, and instead of touching his forehead, as poor children are taught to do, held out his hand and said﻿—

“How do you do, Lord Arden?”

“I am very well, I thank you,” said Lord Arden. “And where did you spring from? You are not a native of these parts, I think?”
===Chinese===
「哦，迪基，这是我爸爸，」埃德雷德叫着，连爬带站地起来。迪基又飞快地补了一句：「我叫迪基·哈丁。」迪基因拄着拐杖，起身慢了些，阿登勋爵（Lord Arden）便俯下身来伸手搀他。这个衣衫寒碜的跛脚孩子站起身来，并不像穷人家孩子受过的教导那样抬手摸一摸前额行礼，反而伸出手来说——他心里想必微微吃了一惊——

「您好，阿登勋爵。」

「我很好，谢谢你，」阿登勋爵说。「你是打哪儿冒出来的？我想你不是本地人吧？」

===Original===
“No, but my adopted father is,” said Dickie, “and I came from London with him, to see his father, who is old Mr. Beale, and we are staying at his cottage.”

Lord Arden sat down beside them on the turf and asked Dickie a good many questions about where he was born, and who he had lived with, and what he had seen and done and been.

Dickie answered honestly and straightforwardly. Only of course he did not tell about the magic, or say that in that magic world he and Lord Arden’s children were friends and cousins. And all the time they were talking Lord Arden’s eyes were fixed on his face, except when they wandered to Tinkler and the white seal. Once he picked these up, and looked at the crest on them.
===Chinese===
「不是，不过我的养父是本地人，」迪基说，「我跟他从伦敦一起来，看望他的父亲——也就是老比尔先生，我们如今就住在他那座村舍里。」

阿登勋爵在草皮上挨着他们坐下，问了迪基许多问题：生在什么地方，一向跟什么人过活，见过些什么，做过些什么，经历过些什么。

迪基答得老老实实、直截了当。当然，魔法的事他只字未提，也没说在那个魔法世界里，他同阿登勋爵的孩子们是朋友，还是表亲。他们交谈的整个过程里，阿登勋爵的目光一直停在他脸上，只有视线偶尔飘到叮铃和白图章上去的时候例外。有一回他把这两样东西拿起来，端详上面的纹章。

===Original===
“Where did you get these?” he asked.

Dickie told. And then Lord Arden handed the seal and Tinkler to him and went on with his questions.

At last Elfrida put her arms round her father’s neck and whispered. “I know it’s not manners, but Dickie won’t mind,” she said before the whispering began.
===Chinese===
「这些东西你是从哪儿得来的？」他问。

迪基讲了。然后阿登勋爵把图章和叮铃递还给他，又接着问他的话。

终于，埃尔弗里达搂住爸爸的脖子，跟他咬起耳朵来。「我知道这不合礼数，可迪基不会介意的，」她在咬耳朵之前先这么声明了一句。

===Original===
“Yes, certainly,” said Lord Arden when the whispering was over; “it’s teatime. Dickie, you’ll come home to tea with us, won’t you?”

“I must tell Mr. Beale,” said Dickie; “he’ll be anxious if I don’t.”

“Shall I hurt you if I put you on my back?” Lord Arden asked, and next minute he was carrying Dickie down the slope towards Arden Castle, while Edred went back to Beale’s cottage to say where Dickie was. When Edred got back to Arden Castle tea was ready in the parlor, and Dickie was resting in a comfortable chair.
===Chinese===
「好，当然，」咬耳朵完了以后，阿登勋爵说；「到喝茶的时辰了。迪基，跟我们回家去喝茶，好不好？」

「我得跟比尔先生说一声，」迪基说；「不然他要着急的。」

「我背你，会硌着你吗？」阿登勋爵问。下一分钟，他已经背着迪基，朝阿登城堡下面的坡走下去了；埃德雷德则折回比尔的村舍，去报个信儿，说迪基在哪儿。埃德雷德回到阿登城堡时，客厅里茶已经备好，迪基正坐在一把舒服的椅子里歇着。

===Original===
“Isn’t old Beale a funny old man?” said Edred. “He said Arden Castle was the right place for Dickie, with a face like that. What could he have meant? What are you doing that for?” he added in injured tones, for Elfrida had kicked his hand under the table.

Before tea was over there was a sound of horses’ hoofs and carriage wheels in the courtyard. And the maidservant opened the parlor door and said, “Lady Talbot.” Though he remembered well enough how kind she had been to him, Dickie wished he could creep under the table. It was too hard; she must recognize him. And now Edred and Elfrida, and Lord Arden, who was so kind and jolly, they would all know that he had once been a burglar, and that she had wanted to adopt him, and that he had been ungrateful and had run away. He trembled all over. It was too hard.
===Chinese===
「老比尔这老头儿是不是挺有意思？」埃德雷德说。「他说，瞧迪基那张脸，阿登城堡才是他的安身之地。他这话能是什么意思呢？你踢我干什么？」他委屈地补了一句，因为埃尔弗里达在桌子底下踢了他的手。

茶还没喝完，院子里响起了马蹄声和车轮声。女仆打开客厅门，通报说：「塔尔博特夫人（Lady Talbot）到。」迪基虽然清清楚楚记得她从前待自己多么好，却恨不得钻到桌子底下去。这太难受了；她准会认出他来。这一来，埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达，还有这位和蔼快活的阿登勋爵，就全都会知道他从前当过贼，知道她想收养他，还知道他不知好歹、逃之夭夭。他浑身直哆嗦。这太难受了。

===Original===
Lady Talbot shook hands with the others, and then turned to him. “And who is your little friend?” she asked Edred, and in the same breath cried out﻿—“Why, it’s my little runaway!”

Dickie only said: “I wasn’t ungrateful, I wasn’t﻿—I had to go.” But his eyes implored.

And Lady Talbot﻿—Dickie will always love her for that﻿—understood. Not a word about burglars did she say, only﻿—
===Chinese===
塔尔博特夫人同大家一一握手，然后转向他。「你这位小朋友是谁呀？」她问埃德雷德，话音未落便失声叫道——「哎呀，这不是我的小逃兵吗！」

迪基只说了一句：「我不是不知好歹，我不是——我非走不可。」可他的眼睛在苦苦哀求。

而塔尔博特夫人——单为这一点，迪基就会永远爱她——懂了。贼呀盗的，她一个字没提，只说——

===Original===
“I wanted to adopt Dickie once, Lord Arden, but he would not stay.”

“I had to get back to father,” said Dickie.

“Well, at any rate it’s pleasant to see each other again,” she said. “I always hoped we should some day. No sugar, thank you, Elfrida”﻿—and then sat down and had tea and was as jolly as possible. The only thing which made Dickie at all uncomfortable was when she turned suddenly to the master of the house and said, “Doesn’t he remind you of anyone, Lord Arden?”
===Chinese===
「我从前一度想收养迪基，阿登勋爵，可他不肯留下来。」

「我非回到爸爸身边不可，」迪基说。

「唉，不管怎么说，重新见面总是叫人高兴的，」她说。「我一直在盼着有这一天呢。不要糖，谢谢，埃尔弗里达」——说完便坐下来喝茶，显得再快活不过。唯一叫迪基有点儿不自在的，是她忽然转向主人说：「他是不是让你想起什么人来了，阿登勋爵？」

===Original===
And Lord Arden said, “Perhaps he does,” with that sort of look that people have when they mean: “Not before the children! I’d rather talk about it afterwards if you don’t mind.”

Then the three were sent out to play, and Dickie was shown the castle ruins, while Lord Arden and Lady Talbot walked up and down on the daisied grass, and talked for a long time. Dickie knew they were talking about him, but he did not mind. He had that feeling you sometimes have about grown-up people, that they really do understand, and are to be trusted.

“You’ll be too fine presently to speak to the likes of us, you nipper,” said Beale, when a smart little pony cart had brought Dickie back to the cottage. “You an’ your grand friends. Lord Arden indeed﻿—”
===Chinese===
阿登勋爵说：「也许是的。」那神情，正是人们心里念着这句话时的神情：「别当着孩子们！要是你不介意，这事回头再谈吧。」

然后三个孩子被打发出去玩，有人领迪基去看城堡的废墟，阿登勋爵和塔尔博特夫人则在开满雏菊的草地上踱来踱去，谈了很长时间。迪基知道他们谈的是自己，可他并不介意。他有一种人对大人们有时会有的感觉：他们真的明白，而且靠得住。

「过不了几天，你就要阔气得懒得搭理我们这号人了，小家伙，」一辆挺神气的小马双轮车把迪基送回村舍时，比尔说。「你呀，还有你那些贵朋友。阿登勋爵，嚯——」

===Original===
“They was as jolly as jolly,” said Dickie; “nobody weren’t never kinder to me nor what Lord Arden was an’ Lady Talbot too﻿—without it was you, farver.”

“Ah,” said Beale to the old man, “ ’e knows how to get round his old father, don’t ’e?”

“What does he want to talk that way for?” the old man asked. “ ’E can talk like a little gentleman all right ’cause we ’eard ’im.”
===Chinese===
「他们呀，和气得没法再和气，」迪基说；「再没有人比阿登勋爵待我更好的了，塔尔博特夫人也一样——除了您，爸爸。」

「瞧见没，」比尔对老人说，「他多会哄他那老爸爸，是不是？」

「他干嘛要那么说话？」老人问。「他明明能像个小小绅士那样说话，我们亲耳听见的。」

===Original===
“Oh, that’s the way we talks up London way,” said Dickie. “I learned to talk fine out o’ books.”

Mr. Beale said nothing, but that night he actually read for nearly ten minutes in a bound volume of the *Wesleyan Magazine*. And he was asleep over the same entertaining work when Lord Arden came the next afternoon.

You will be able to guess what he came about. And Dickie had a sort of feeling that perhaps Lord Arden might have seen by his face, as old Beale had, that he was an Arden. So neither he nor you will be much surprised. The person to be really surprised was Mr. Beale.
===Chinese===
「哦，那是咱们伦敦那头的说话法儿，」迪基说。「我是打书本里学来的斯文话。」

比尔先生没说什么，可就在那天晚上，他当真捧着一本合订的《卫斯理公会杂志》（Wesleyan Magazine），读了差不多十分钟之久。第二天下午阿登勋爵来的时候，他还伏在这份有趣的读物上睡着了呢。

你能猜到他来是为了什么。迪基心里也隐隐觉得，也许阿登勋爵跟老比尔一样，看他的脸就看出了他是个阿登家的人。所以无论他还是你们，都不会大惊小怪。真正要大吃一惊的，是比尔先生。

===Original===
“You might a-knocked me down with a pickaxe,” said Beale later, “so help me three men and a boy you might. It’s a rum go. My lord ’e says there’s some woman been writing letters to ’im this long time saying she’d got ’old of ’is long-lost nephew or cousin or something, and a-wanting to get money out of him﻿—though what for, goodness knows. An’ ’e says you’re a Arden by rights, you nipper you, an’ ’e wants to take you and bring you up along of his kids﻿—so there’s an end of you and me, Dickie, old boy. I didn’t understand more than ’arf of wot ’e was saying. But I tumbled to that much. It’s all up with you and me and Amelia and the dogs and the little ’ome. You’re a-goin’ to be a gentleman, you are﻿—an’ I’ll have to take to the road by meself and be a poor beast of a cadger again. That’s what it’ll come to, I know.”

“Don’t you put yourself about,” said Dickie calmly. “I ain’t a-goin’ to leave yer. Didn’t Lady Talbot ask me to be her boy﻿—and didn’t I cut straight back to you? I’ll play along o’ them kids if Lord Arden’ll let me. But I ain’t a-goin’ to leave you, not yet I ain’t. So don’t you go snivelling afore anyone’s ’urt yer, farver. See?”
===Chinese===
「拿一把镐头就能把我敲倒，」比尔后来这么说，「我拿三个人带一个小娃起誓，真的能。这事儿透着邪门儿。勋爵老爷说，有个女人这老长时间一直给他写信，说自己寻见了他失散多年的侄子还是表侄什么的，想从他身上弄钱——图个什么，只有天知道。他还说，你小子呀，按理就是个阿登家的人，他要接你去，跟他的孩子们一块儿养大——得，你我爷儿俩就算到头了，迪基，我的老伙计。他的话我懂了一半不到。可就这一点我听明白了。你跟我，还有阿米莉亚，还有狗，还有咱们的小家，全完了。你就要做绅士了，你呀——我呢，只好独自一个人重上路子，再去当那可怜巴巴的讨饭牲口。我知道，早晚就是这个下场。」

「您别自个儿吓自个儿，」迪基不慌不忙地说。「我不离开您。塔尔博特夫人不是要我给她当儿子吗——我不是扭头就奔回您这儿来了？阿登勋爵要是肯，我就跟那些孩子们一块儿玩玩。可我不离开您，眼下还不离开。所以您别还没人伤着您呢，就先哭鼻子，爸爸。听见没？」

===Original===
But that was before Lord Arden had his second talk with Mr. Beale. After that it was﻿—

“Look ’ere, you nipper, I ain’t a-goin’ to stand in your light. You’re goin’ up in the world, says you. Well, you ain’t the only one. Lord Arden’s bought father’s cottage an’ ’e’s goin’ to build on to it, and I’m to ’ave all the dawgs down ’ere, and sell ’em through the papers like. And you’ll come an’ ’ave a look at us sometimes.”

“And what about Amelia?” said Dickie, “and the little ones?”
===Chinese===
不过，这是在阿登勋爵同比尔先生第二次谈话之前的话。那次谈话之后，话就成了这样——

「听着，小家伙，我不能挡你的亮。你说你要高升了。哼，高升的又不光你一个。阿登勋爵把我爹的村舍买了下来，还要往上扩建，这儿所有的狗都归我管，往后还能在报上登广告卖狗。你嘛，偶尔也回来看看我们。」

「那阿米莉亚（Amelia）怎么办？」迪基说，「还有那些小的们呢？」

===Original===
“Well, I did think,” said Beale, rubbing his nose thoughtfully, “of asking ’Melia to come down ’ere along o’ the dawgs. Seems a pity to separate ’em somehow. It was Lord Arden put it into my ’ed. ‘You oughter be married you ought,’ ’e says to me pleasant like, man to man; ‘ain’t there any young woman I could give a trifle to, to set you and her up in housekeeping?’ So then I casts about, and I thinks of ’Melia. As well ’er as anybody, and she’s used to the dawgs. And the trifle’s an hundred pounds. That’s all. *That’s all!* So I’m sending to her by this post, and it’s an awful toss up getting married, but ’Melia ain’t like a stranger, and it couldn’t ever be the same with us two and nipper after all this set out. What you say?”

I don’t know what Dickie said; what he felt was something like this:﻿—

“I *have* tried to stick to Beale, and help him along, and I did come back from the other old long-ago world to help him, and I have been sticking to things I didn’t like so as to help him and get him settled. He was my bit of work, and now someone else comes along and takes my work out of my hands, and finishes it. And here’s Beale provided for and settled. And I meant to provide for him myself. And I don’t like it!”
===Chinese===
「这个嘛，我倒是琢磨过，」比尔若有所思地揉着鼻子说，「想请米莉亚（’Melia）跟狗一块儿下来住。不知怎么的，把她们拆开总有点可惜。这主意还是阿登勋爵给塞进我脑袋里的。『你呀，早该成个家了，』他和和气气地、像男人对男人那样跟我说；『有没有哪位姑娘，我可以出笔小钱给她，好让你跟她立起个家来？』于是我盘算来盘算去，就想起了米莉亚。跟谁结都一样，况且她跟狗也处惯了。那笔小钱是一百英镑。就这么回事。*就这么回事！*所以我今天就给她去信。结婚这桩事凶吉难卜，可米莉亚总不算外人；再说闹了这么一场之后，咱们爷儿俩再想跟从前一样，也是不可能的了。你说呢？」

迪基说了什么，我不知道；他心里的滋味，大约是这样的——

「*我*是真心实意守着比尔的，帮他往前奔，我还真的从那古老久远的另一个世界回来帮过他，为了帮他、给他安顿下一个家，我一直咬牙忍着种种不喜欢的事。他本是我自己的一份功课，如今却来了旁人，把我的功课从我手里接了过去，替我做完了。比尔有着落了，安顿好了。可我原打算亲手给他安顿的。我不乐意，我就是不乐意！」

===Original===
That was what he felt at first. But afterwards he had to own that it was “a jolly lucky thing for Beale.” And for himself too. He found that to be at Arden Castle with Edred and Elfrida all day, at play and at lessons, was almost as good as being with them in the beautiful old dream-life. All the things that he had hated in this modern life, when he was Dickie of Deptford, ceased to trouble him now that he was Richard Arden. For the difference between being rich and poor is as great as the difference between being warm and cold.

After that first day a sort of shyness came over the three children, and they spoke no more of the strange adventures they had had together, but just played at all the ordinary everyday games, till they almost forgot that there was any magic, had ever been any. The fact was, the life they were leading was so happy in itself that they needed no magic to make them contented. It was not till after the wedding of ’Melia and Mr. Beale that Dickie remembered that to find the Arden Treasure for his cousins had been one of his reasons for coming back to this, the Nowadays world.

I wish I had time to tell you about the wedding. I could write a whole book about it. How Amelia came down from London and was married in Arden Church. How she wore a white dress and a large hat with a wreath of orange blossoms, a filmy veil, and real kid gloves﻿—all gifts of Miss Edith Arden, Lord Arden’s sister. How Lord Arden presented an enormous wedding cake and a glorious wedding breakfast, and gave away the bride, and made a speech saying he owed a great debt to Mr. Beale for his kindness to his nephew Richard Arden, and how surprised everyone was to hear Dickie’s new name. How all the dogs wore white favors and had each a crumb of wedding cake; and how when the wedding feast was over and the guests gone, the bride tucked up her white dress under a big apron and set about arranging in the new rooms the “sticks” of furniture which Dickie and Beale had brought together from the little home in Deptford, and which had come in a van by road all the way to Arden.
===Chinese===
起初，他就是这么想的。可后来他不得不承认，这「实在是比尔天大的福气」。对他自己也是。他发现，整天在阿登城堡里同埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达一起玩耍、一起上课，几乎就跟在美好的旧梦里同他们在一起一样快活。他做德普特福德（Deptford）的迪基的时候，在这现代生活里恨透了的种种，如今他做了理查德·阿登（Richard Arden），便再不能搅扰他了。因为富与贫的差别，就跟暖与冷的差别一样大。

头一天过后，一种羞怯劲儿悄悄爬上三个孩子的心头，他们不再提起一起经历过的种种奇遇，只玩些平平常常、天天都玩的游戏，玩得几乎忘了世上有什么魔法，忘了曾经有过。实情是，他们眼下的日子本身就够快乐的，用不着魔法来添什么心满意足。直到米莉亚同比尔先生办完婚事，迪基才又想起来：替表亲们找到阿登家的宝藏，本是他回到这个当今世界来的缘由之一。

我真希望有工夫把那场婚礼讲给你们听。单是它就够写一整本书的。讲阿米莉亚怎么从伦敦下来，在阿登教堂里成了亲；她怎么穿着一身白裙，戴一顶插着橙花花环的大帽子，蒙着薄如轻烟的面纱，戴着真正的羔羊皮手套——全是阿登勋爵的妹妹伊迪丝·阿登小姐（Miss Edith Arden）送的；讲阿登勋爵怎么献上一座大得惊人的婚礼蛋糕和一席辉煌的婚宴早餐，怎么把新娘交到新郎手里，还致了辞，说比尔先生待他的侄子理查德·阿登恩重如山，他感激不尽；又讲大伙儿听见迪基的新名字时多么吃惊。讲所有的狗都戴上白色花结，每一只还分到一小屑婚礼蛋糕；又讲喜宴散了、宾客走了之后，新娘把白裙掖进一条大围裙底下，动手拾掇起新居的房间，安放那些家什——那是迪基和比尔从德普特福德那个小家里一点点凑起来的，用大篷车走旱路，一路运到了阿登。

===Original===
The Ardens had gone back to the Castle, and Dickie with them, and old Beale was smoking in his usual chair by his front door﻿—so there was no one to hear Beale’s compliment to his bride. He came behind her and put his arm round her as she was dusting the mantelpiece. “Go on with you,” said the new Mrs. Beale; “anyone ’ud think we was courting.”

“So we be,” said Beale, and kissed ’Melia for the first time. “We got all our courtin’ to do now. See? I might a-picked an’ choosed,” he added reflectively, “but there﻿—I dare say I might a-done worse.”

’Melia blushed with pleasure at the compliment, and went on with the dusting.
===Chinese===
阿登一家人回城堡去了，迪基也跟了去；老比尔在他前门口他惯坐的那把椅子上抽着烟——所以比尔对他新娘说的那句恭维话，没有旁人听见。她正掸着壁炉架上的灰，他绕到她背后，伸胳膊搂住了她。「去你的吧，」新比尔太太说；「人家还当咱们在谈情说爱呢。」

「咱们本来就是在谈着呢，」比尔说着，头一回吻了米莉亚。「咱们的谈情说爱，如今才正要开始呢。懂不？我原本也可以挑三拣四的，」他若有所思地补了一句，「不过话说回来——再挑，也未必挑得比这强。」

这句恭维话叫米莉亚乐得满脸飞红，她接着掸她的灰。

===Original===
---

It was as the Ardens walked home over the short turf that Lord Arden said to his sister, “I wish all the cottages about here were like Beale’s. It didn’t cost so very much. If I could only buy back the rest of the land, I’d show some people what a model village is like. Only I can’t buy it back. He wants far more than we can think of managing.”

And Dickie heard what he said. That was why, when next he was alone with his cousins, he began﻿—

“Look here﻿—you aren’t allowed to use your magic any more, to go and look for the treasure. But *I* am. And I vote we go and look for it. And then your father can buy back the old lands, and build the new cottages and mend up Arden Castle, and make it like it used to be.”
===Chinese===
---

阿登一家人踩着短短的草皮步行回家时，阿登勋爵对他妹妹说：「但愿这一带的村舍都盖得跟比尔家的一样。那并花不了多少钱。只要我能把其余的田产买回来，我倒要叫某些人瞧瞧，一个模范村庄是什么样子。只是我买不回来。他开的价，远远超出我们盘算得起的数目。」

这些话，迪基听见了。所以，下一回他跟表亲们单独在一起的时候，他就开了口——

「你们听着——你们是不许再用魔法去寻宝藏了。可*我*不在此列。我提议咱们去把它找出来。这样，你们爸爸就能买回旧日的田产，盖起新的村舍，把阿登城堡修好，叫它恢复旧观。」

===Original===
“Oh, let’s,” said Elfrida, with enthusiasm. But Edred unexpectedly answered, “I don’t know.” The three children were sitting in the window of the gate-tower looking down on the green turf of the Castle yard.

“What do you mean you don’t know?” Elfrida asked briskly.

“I *mean* I don’t know,” said Edred stolidly; “we’re all right as we are, *I* think. I used to think I liked magic and things. But if you come to think of it something horrid happened to us every single time we went into the past with our magic. We were always being chased or put in prison or bothered somehow or other. The only really nice thing was when we saw the treasure being hidden, because that looked like a picture and we hadn’t to do anything. And we don’t know where the treasure is, anyhow. And I don’t like adventures nearly so much as I used to think I did. We’re all right and jolly as we are. What I say is, ‘Don’t let’s.’ ”
===Chinese===
「哦，去寻吧，」埃尔弗里达热情地说。不料埃德雷德却答了一句：「我说不好。」三个孩子正坐在门楼的窗口，俯望着城堡院子里的绿草地。

「你说不好，是什么意思？」埃尔弗里达干脆利落地问。

「*我的意思是*我说不好，」埃德雷德直挺挺地说；「*我看*咱们眼下这样挺好。我从前一直以为自己喜欢魔法那一套。可你仔细想想，咱们每回带着魔法回到过去，准出一件吓人的事。不是被追，就是被关进监牢，再不然总有什么麻烦找上门。唯一真正美的一回，是亲眼看见宝藏被藏起来，因为那就像看一幅画，咱们什么也不用干。再说宝藏到底在哪儿，咱们也不知道。还有，冒险这事儿，也远不如我从前自己想的那么合我胃口。咱们眼下这样又自在又快活。要我说，就一句话：『咱们别去。』」

===Original===
This cold water damped the spirit of the others only for a few minutes.

“You know,” Elfrida explained to Dickie, “our magic took us to look for treasure in the past. And once a film of a photograph that we’d stuck up behaved like a cinematograph, and then we saw the treasure being hidden away.”

“Then let’s just go where that was﻿—mark the spot, come home and then dig it up.”
===Chinese===
这盆冷水，也只把另外两个的兴致浇凉了几分钟。

「是这样的，」埃尔弗里达对迪基解释，「我们的魔法带我们回到过去寻宝藏。有一回，我们贴在墙上的一张照片底片忽然像放活动电影似的演起来，我们就看见了宝藏被藏进去的情景。」

「那就单去那个地方——把位置记下，回家来再把它挖出来。」

===Original===
“It wasn’t buried,” Elfrida explained; “it was put into a sort of cellar, with doors, and we’ve looked all over what’s left of the Castle, and there isn’t so much as a teeny silver ring to be found.”

“I see,” said Dickie. “But suppose I just worked the magic and wished to be where the treasure is?”

“I won’t,” cried Edred, and in his extreme dislike to the idea he kicked with his boots quite violently against the stones of the tower; “not much I won’t. I expect the treasure’s bricked up. We should look nice bricked up in a vault like a wicked nun, and perhaps forgotten the way to get out. Not much.”
===Chinese===
「那宝藏不是埋着的，」埃尔弗里达解释；「它是搁在一种地窖里的，有门；我们把城堡剩下的每一处都翻遍了，连一枚顶小顶小的银戒指也没找见。」

「明白了，」迪基说。「可要是我只管施一回魔法，只求到宝藏待着的地方去呢？」

「我不去，」埃德雷德叫起来，他对这主意反感到了极点，穿着靴子狠狠地踢着门楼的石头；「说什么我也不去。我猜宝藏八成给砌死了。咱们要是给砌在墓窖里头，活像个小坏修女，那才好看呢，说不定还忘了出去的路。想都别想。」

===Original===
“You needn’t make such a fuss about it,” said Elfrida, “nobody’s going to get bricked up in vaults.” And Dickie added, “You’re quite right, old chap. I didn’t think about that.”

“We must do *something*,” Elfrida said impatiently.

“How would it be,” Dickie spoke slowly, “if I tried to see the Mouldierwarp? He is stronger than the Mouldiwarp. He might advise us. Suppose we work the magic and just ask to see him?”
===Chinese===
「你也犯不上这么大惊小怪，」埃尔弗里达说，「谁也不会叫人砌进墓窖里去的。」迪基跟着说：「你说得一点不错，老伙计。这个我没想到。」

「咱们总得*干点什么*呀，」埃尔弗里达不耐烦地说。

「要是我设法见一见莫迪尔瓦普（Mouldierwarp），」迪基慢慢地说，「会怎么样？他比莫德瓦普（Mouldiwarp）更强。他兴许能指点咱们。咱们施起魔法来，只求见他一面，怎么样？」

===Original===
“I don’t want to go away from here,” said Edred firmly.

“You needn’t. I’ll lay out the moonseeds and things on the floor here﻿—you’ll see.”

So Dickie made the crossed triangles of moonseeds and he and his cousins stood in it and Dickie said, “Please can we see the Mouldierwarp?” just as you say, “Please can I see Mr. So-and-so?” when you have knocked at the door of Mr. So-and-so’s house and someone has opened the door.
===Chinese===
「我不想离开这儿，」埃德雷德斩钉截铁地说。

「用不着离开。我就在这儿的地上摆开月籽和那些东西——你等着瞧吧。」

于是迪基用月籽摆出交叠的三角，他和表亲们站了进去，迪基说：「请问，我们能见一见莫迪尔瓦普吗？」——就像你敲了某先生家的门、有人开了门之后，你说「请问，我能见一见某先生吗」一模一样。

===Original===
Immediately everything became dark, but before the children had time to wish that it was light again a disc of light appeared on the curtain of darkness, and there was the Mouldierwarp, just as Dickie had seen him once before.

He bowed in a courtly manner, and said﻿—

“What can I do for you today, Richard Lord Arden?”
===Chinese===
立时四下里黑了下来，可孩子们还没来得及盼它重新亮起来，黑暗的帷幕上就现出一轮光的圆盘，莫迪尔瓦普就在那上面，同迪基从前见过的那一次一模一样。

他彬彬有礼地一躬身，说道——

「今天我能为您效什么劳，理查德·阿登勋爵？」

===Original===
“He’s not Lord Arden,” said Edred. “*I* used to be. But even *I’m* not Lord Arden now. My father is.”

“Indeed?” said the Mouldierwarp with an air of polite interest. “You interest me greatly. But my question remains unanswered.”

“I want,” said Dickie, “to find the lost treasure of Arden, so that the old Castle can be built up again, and the old lands bought back, and the old cottages made pretty and good to live in. Will you please advise me?”
===Chinese===
「他不是阿登勋爵，」埃德雷德说。「*我*从前是。可如今连*我*也不是阿登勋爵了。我爸爸才是。」

「哦？」莫迪尔瓦普带着一副客客气气、颇感兴趣的神气说。「你们的话叫我深感兴趣。可我的问题还没有得到回答呢。」

「我想，」迪基说，「找到阿登家失落的宝藏，好让旧城堡重新盖起来，旧田产买了回来，旧村舍修得漂漂亮亮、住着舒舒服服。请您指点我，行吗？」

===Original===
The Mouldierwarp in the magic-lantern picture seemed to scratch his nose thoughtfully with his fore paw.

“It can be done,” he said, “but it will be hard. It is almost impossible to find the treasure without waking the Mouldiestwarp, who sits on the green-and-white checkered field of Ardens’ shield of arms. And he can only be awakened by some noble deed. Yet noble deeds may chance at any time. And if you go to seek treasure of one kind you may find treasure of another. I have spoken.”

It began to fade away, but Elfrida cried, “Oh, *don’t* go. You’re just like the Greek oracles. Won’t you tell us something plain and straightforward?”

“I will,” said the Mouldierwarp, rather shortly.
===Chinese===
幻灯画片上的莫迪尔瓦普，似乎抬起前爪，若有所思地搔了搔鼻子。

「这事做得成，」他说，「可是难。若不惊醒莫迪斯特瓦普（Mouldiestwarp），宝藏几乎没法找到；他就坐在阿登家盾徽上绿白格纹的底面上。而唯有高尚的行为才能把他惊醒。然而高尚的行为，随时都可能遇上。你们若去寻一种宝藏，或许会寻到另一种。吾言已毕。」

那形象开始淡下去，埃尔弗里达忙喊：「哦，*别*走。您跟希腊的神谕一个样。就不能给我们说一句明明白白、直截了当的话吗？」

「可以，」莫迪尔瓦普说，口气相当生硬。

===Original===
> “Great Arden’s Lord no treasure shall regain

 Till Arden’s Lord is lost and found again.”

“And father *was* lost and found again,” said Edred, “so that’s all right.”

> “Set forth to seek it with courageous face.

 And seek it in the most unlikely place.”
===Chinese===
> 「伟大的阿登之主，休想重得宝藏——

> 除非阿登之主失散流离，复又寻回。」

「爸爸*确实*失散过又寻回来了呀，」埃德雷德说，「所以这一条不碍事。」

> 「且鼓起无畏的面容，动身去寻，

> 到那最不可能的地方去寻。」

===Original===
And with that it vanished altogether, and the darkness with it; and there were the three children and Tinkler and the white seal and the moonseeds and the sunshine on the floor of the room in the tower.

“That’s useful,” said Edred scornfully. “As if it wasn’t just as difficult to know the unlikely places as the likely ones.”

“I’ll tell you what,” said Dickie. And then the dinner bell rang, and they had to go without Dickie’s telling them what, and to eat roast mutton and plum-pie, and behave as though they were just ordinary children to whom no magic had ever happened. There was little chance of more talk that day.
===Chinese===
话音一落，那形象整个儿消失了，黑暗也随之散去；塔楼那间屋子的地板上，只有三个孩子，和叮铃、白图章、月籽，还有一片阳光。

「这可真管用，」埃德雷德讥诮地说。「说得倒轻巧，好像知道哪儿『不可能』，就比知道哪儿『可能』容易似的。」

「我来告诉你们一个主意，」迪基说。可就在这时，开饭铃响了，他们只好没听迪基说出那个主意就去吃饭：吃烤羊肉和梅子馅饼，还得一举一动都像普普通通、跟魔法从没打过交道的孩子。这一天，再想多谈是没有机会了。

===Original===
Edred and Elfrida were to be taken to Cliffville immediately after dinner to be measured for new shoes, and Dickie was to go up to spend the afternoon with Beale and ’Melia and the dogs. Still, in the few moments when they were all dressed and waiting for the dogcart to come round, Dickie found a chance to whisper to Elfrida﻿—

“Let’s all think of unlikely places as hard as ever we can. And tomorrow we’ll decide on the unlikeliest and go there. Edred needn’t be in it if he doesn’t want to. *You’re* keen, aren’t you?”

“Rather!” was all there was time for Elfrida to say.
===Chinese===
一吃过饭，就要有人带埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达上克利夫维尔（Cliffville）去量脚做新鞋；迪基则要上去，同比尔、米莉亚和狗们一起消磨下午。不过，在他们全都穿戴整齐、等着马车兜过来的几分钟里，迪基还是找到机会跟埃尔弗里达咬耳朵——

「咱们都使劲想想那些最不可能的地方，能想多狠想多狠。明天咱们挑出那个最不可能的，就上那儿去。埃德雷德要是不乐意，可以不入伙。*你*是起劲的，对不对？」

「起劲极了！」埃尔弗里达只来得及说这么一句。

===Original===
The welcome that awaited Dickie at Beale’s cottage from Beale, Amelia, and, not least, the dogs, was enough to drive all thoughts of unlikely places out of anybody’s head. And besides, there were always so many interesting things to do at the cottage. He helped to wash True, cleaned the knives, and rinsed lettuce for tea; helped to dry the tea-things, and to fold the washing when Mrs. Beale brought it in out of the yard in dry, sweet armfuls of white folds.

It was dusk when he bade them good night, embracing each dog in turn, and set out to walk the little way to the crossroads, where the dogcart returning from Cliffville would pick him up. But the dogcart was a little late, because the pony had dropped a shoe and had had to be taken to the blacksmith’s.

So when Dickie had waited a little while he began to think, as one always does when people don’t keep their appointments, that perhaps he had mistaken the time, or that the clock at the cottage was slow. And when he had waited a little longer, it seemed simply silly to be waiting at all. So he picked up his crutch and got up from the milestone where he had been sitting and set off to walk down to the Castle.
===Chinese===
比尔村舍里，比尔、阿米莉亚，尤其是狗们，迎接迪基的那份热乎劲儿，足够把任何人脑子里关于「不可能的地方」的念头都赶得一干二净。再说，村舍里总有那么多有趣的事可做。他帮忙给特鲁（True）洗澡，擦了刀，洗了做茶用的莴苣；帮着擦干茶具，等比尔太太从院子里抱进一抱又一抱干爽喷香的白床单、白衣裳，就帮着叠起来。

他向大家道了晚安，轮流搂过每只狗亲了亲，动身走上那一小段路，到十字路口去等从克利夫维尔回来的马车顺路捎他，这时天已经擦黑了。可马车误了点，因为小马掉了一只蹄铁，得牵到铁匠铺去。

于是迪基等了一会儿，就像人们遇上别人失约时总要起的那种疑心一样，开始琢磨：也许是自己把钟点记错了，或者是村舍里的钟慢了。又多等了一会儿，他觉得这等待简直是犯傻。于是他拾起拐杖，从坐着的路碑上站起身，朝城堡的方向走下坡去。

===Original===
As he went he thought many things, and one of the things he thought was that the memories of King James’s time had grown dim and distant﻿—he looked down on Arden Castle and loved it, and felt that he asked no better than to live there all his life with his cousins and their father, and that, after all, the magic of a dream-life was not needed, when life itself was so good and happy.

And just as he was thinking this a twig cracked sharply in the hedge. Then a dozen twigs rustled and broke, and something like a great black bird seemed to fly out at him and fold him in its wings.

It was not a bird﻿—he knew that the next moment﻿—but a big, dark cloak, that someone had thrown over his head and shoulders, and through it strong hands were holding him.
===Chinese===
一面走，他想了许多事，其中一件是：詹姆士国王（King James）时代的那些记忆，已经变得又模糊又遥远——他俯望着阿登城堡，满心爱它，只觉得但求一辈子同表亲们和他们的父亲住在那里，便别无所求；又说到底，人生本身既然这样美好快乐，梦的生涯那种魔法也就用不着了。

正想着，树篱里一根细枝「咔」地一声脆响。接着十几根细枝窸窸窣窣地折断，仿佛一只大黑鸟扑到他身上，把他裹进了翅膀。

那不是鸟——下一刻他就明白了——而是一件又大又黑的斗篷，被人兜头罩住了他的脑袋和肩膀；斗篷底下，几只有力的手紧紧攥着他。

===Original===
“Hold yer noise!” said a voice; “if you so much as squeak it’ll be the worse for you.”

“Help!” shouted Dickie instantly.

He was thrown on to the ground. Hands fumbled, his face was cleared of the cloak, and a handkerchief with a round pebble in it was stuffed into his mouth so that he could not speak. Then he was dragged behind a hedge and held there, while two voices whispered above him. The cloak was over his head again now, and he could see nothing, but he could hear. He heard one of the voices say, “Hush! they’re coming.” And then he heard the sound of hoofs and wheels, and Lord Arden’s jolly voice saying, “He must have walked on; we shall catch him up all right.” Then the sound of wheels and hoofs died away, and hard hands pulled him to his feet and thrust the crutch under his arm.
===Chinese===
「不许出声！」一个声音说；「你敢吱半声，就有你的好看。」

「救命！」迪基立刻大声喊。

他被摔在地上。几只手摸索着，把斗篷从他脸上掀开，又把一方裹着圆石子的手帕塞进他嘴里，叫他出不得声。然后他被拖到一道树篱后面按住，两个声音在他头顶上窃窃私语。斗篷重又罩上了他的头，他什么也看不见，耳朵却听得见。他听见其中一个声音说：「嘘！他们来了。」随后他听见蹄声和车轮声，听见阿登勋爵那快活的大嗓门说：「他一定是往前走了，咱们准能追上他。」再后来，车轮声和蹄声渐渐远去，几只硬邦邦的手把他拽起来，把拐杖塞到他腋下。

===Original===
“Step out!” said one of the voices, “and step out sharp﻿—see?﻿—or I’ll l’arn you! There’s a carriage awaiting for you.”

He stepped out; there was nothing else to be done. They had taken the cloak from his eyes now, and he saw presently that they were nearing a coster’s barrow.

They laid him in the barrow, covered him with the cloak, and put vegetable marrows and cabbages on that. They only left him a little room to breathe.
===Chinese===
「走快些！」其中一个声音说，「麻利点儿走——听见没？——不然我就给你上一课！有辆马车等着你呢。」

他走快了；此外没有别的办法。这会儿他们已把斗篷从他眼睛上摘了下来，不多时他便看出，他们正朝着一辆菜贩的手推车走近。

他们把他放进手推车，用斗篷盖上，又在上头压了西葫芦和卷心菜，只给他留出喘气的那么一小点儿空。

===Original===
“Now lie still for your life!” said the second voice. “If you stir a inch I’ll lick you till you can’t stand! And now you know.”

So he lay still, rigid with misery and despair. For neither of these voices was strange to him. He knew them both only too well.
===Chinese===
「老实躺着，千万躺稳了！」第二个声音说。「你敢动一寸，我就揍到你站不起来！这回记住了。」

他就这样躺着，痛苦和绝望把他冻僵了。因为这两个声音，他一个也不陌生。他太熟悉它们了——熟得不能再熟。

## List of Illustrations / 插图清单

===Original===
1. A six-pointed star pattern made up of two overlapping triangles.

===Chinese===
1. 由两个相互交叠的三角形构成的六芒星图案。

## XI Lord Arden / 第十一章 阿登勋爵

===Original===
There was a lot of talk and a lot of letter-writing before anyone seemed to be able to be sure who was Lord Arden. If the father of Edred and Elfrida had wanted to dispute about it no doubt there would have been enough work to keep the lawyers busy for years, and seas of ink would have been spilled and thunders of eloquence spent on the question. But as the present Lord Arden was an honest man and only too anxious that Dickie should have everything that belonged to him, even the lawyers had to cut their work short.

When Edred saw how his father tried his best to find out the truth about Dickie’s birth, and how willing he was to give up what he had thought was his own, if it should prove to be *not* his, do you think he was not glad to know that he had done his duty, and rescued his cousin, and had not, by any meanness or any indecision, brought dishonor on the name of Arden? As for Elfrida, when she knew the whole story of that night of rescue, she admired her brother so much that it made him almost uncomfortable. However, she now looked up to him in all things and consulted him about everything, and, after all, this is very pleasant from your sister, especially when everyone has been rather in the habit of suggesting that she is better than you are, as well as cleverer.

To Dickie Lord Arden said, “Of course, if anything *should* happen to show that I am really Lord Arden, you won’t desert us, Dickie. You shall go to school with Edred and be brought up like my very own son.”

===Chinese===
为了确定究竟谁才是阿登勋爵，人们谈论了许多，也写了许多信。倘若埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达的父亲存心要在这件事上争一争，无疑够律师们忙上好几年，海一样的墨水要泼洒出去，雷鸣般的雄辩也要耗在这一桩公案上。可如今的这位阿登勋爵是个老实人，一心只盼迪基（Dickie）把他名分下的东西一样不少地拿回去，连律师们也不得不把活儿收了尾。

埃德雷德眼看父亲竭尽全力去查明迪基身世的真相，眼看父亲只要证实那原本以为自己所有的家业*并非*他的，便甘愿放手，你想，他难道不为自己尽了本分、救回了表哥、没有因任何卑劣或任何迟疑而玷辱阿登的姓氏而高兴吗？至于埃尔弗里达（Elfrida），等她知道了那个搭救之夜的整个经过，她对哥哥佩服得五体投地，倒叫哥哥几乎不好意思起来。不过打这以后，她事事都敬着他，样样都去请教他；说到底，妹妹这样待你，是很受用的——尤其是一向人人都习惯了暗示她比你更可爱、也更伶俐的时候。

阿登勋爵对迪基说：「当然，万一有什么*果真*证明我才是真正的阿登勋爵，你可不能抛开我们，迪基。你跟埃德雷德一起去上学，就当我亲生的儿子一样教养成人。」

===Original===
And, like Lord Arden’s very own son, Dickie lived at the house in Arden Castle, and grew to love it more and more. He no longer wanted to get away from these present times to those old days when James the First was King. The times you are born in are always more homelike than any other times can be. When Dickie lived miserably at Deptford he always longed to go to those old times, as a man who is unhappy at home may wish to travel to other countries. But a man who is happy in his home does not want to leave it. And at Arden Dickie was happy. The training he had had in the old-world life enabled him to take his place and to be unembarrassed with the Ardens and their friends as he was with the Beales and theirs. “A little shy,” the Ardens’ friends told each other, “but what fine manners! And to think he was only a tramp! Lord Arden has certainly done wonders with him!”

So Lord Arden got the credit of all that Dickie had learned from his tutors in James the First’s time.

It is not in the nature of any child to brood continually on the past or the future. The child lives in the present. And Dickie lived at Arden and loved it, and enjoyed himself; and Lord Arden bought him a pony, so that his lame foot was hardly any drag at all. The other children had a donkey-cart, and the three made all sorts of interesting expeditions.

Once they went over to Talbot Court, and saw the secret place where Edward Talbot had hidden his confession about having stolen the Arden baby, three generations before. Also they saw the portrait of the Lady Talbot who had been a Miss Arden. In rose-colored brocade she was, with a green silk petticoat and her powdered hair dressed high over a great cushion, but her eyes and her mouth were the eyes of Dickie of Deptford.

===Chinese===
于是，像阿登勋爵的亲生儿子一样，迪基住进了阿登城堡（Arden Castle）的宅子里，一天比一天爱它。他不再想逃离眼下的时代，回到詹姆斯一世做国王的那些旧年月里去。你生在其中的时代，总归比任何别的时代都更像家。当年迪基在德普特福德（Deptford）苦熬的时候，他一心向往那些旧时代，就像在家里过不快活的人向往去别国游历；可一个人若在家中过得快活，就舍不得离开它了。而在阿登，迪基过得快活。他在旧世界生活里受的那一番熏陶，使他能站稳自己的位置，同阿登家人及其朋友们相处自如，就像同比尔先生（Mr. Beale）一家及其朋友们相处一样。「有几分腼腆，」阿登家的朋友们彼此议论，「可这礼数多周正！想想吧，他原本不过是个小流浪汉！阿登勋爵在他身上真是创造了奇迹！」

迪基在詹姆斯一世时代从几位导师那里学到的一切，功劳就这样全记在了阿登勋爵的账上。

没有哪个孩子的天性会没完没了地惦念过去或未来。孩子活在当下。迪基住在阿登，爱着它，乐在其中；阿登勋爵还给他买了一匹小马，那条瘸腿便几乎不再是什么拖累。另外两个孩子有一辆驴车，三个人便做出种种有趣的远行来。

有一回他们过河去了塔尔博特府（Talbot Court），见到了那处密藏的地方——三代之前，爱德华·塔尔博特（Edward Talbot）曾把自己偷走阿登家婴儿的供状藏在里面。他们也见到了塔尔博特夫人的画像，她未出嫁时原是位阿登家的小姐。画上她穿一件玫瑰红的织锦袍，配着绿绸衬裙，扑了粉的头发架在大大的垫托上高高挽起；可她的眼睛、她的嘴，分明就是德普特福德的迪基的眼睛。

===Original===
Lady Talbot was very charming to the children, played hide-and-seek with them, and gave them a delightful and varied tea in the yew arbor.

“I’m glad you wouldn’t let me adopt you, Richard,” she said, when Elfrida and Edred had been sent to her garden to get a basket of peaches to take home with them, “because just when I had become entirely attached to you, you would have found out your real relations, and where would your poor foster-mother have been then?”

“If I could have stayed with you I would,” said Dickie seriously. “I did like you most awfully, even then. You are very like the Lady Arden whose husband was shut up in the Tower for the Gunpowder Plot.”

“So they tell me,” said Lady Talbot, “but how do you know it?”

“I don’t know,” said Dickie confused, “but you *are* like her.”

“You must have seen a portrait of her. There’s one in the National Portrait Gallery. She was a Delamere, and my name was Delamere, too, before I was married. She was one of the same family, you see, dear.”

===Chinese===
塔尔博特夫人（Lady Talbot）待孩子们极和气，跟他们捉迷藏，还在紫杉凉亭里请他们吃了一顿又丰盛又可口、花样百出的茶点。

「理查德（Richard），你不肯让我收养你，我倒觉得庆幸，」她说——这话是在埃尔弗里达和埃德雷德被打发去她园里摘一篮桃子带回家之后——「因为等我对你舍不得放手了，你却会找见你真正的亲人，那时候你可怜的养母可往哪儿搁呢？」

「当初要是能留下来，我是愿意的，」迪基认真地说。「就连那时候，我也着实喜欢你顶厉害的。你很像那位阿登夫人——她的丈夫就是为火药阴谋给关进伦敦塔的。」

「人家都这么说，」塔尔博特夫人说，「可你是怎么知道的呢？」

「我不知道，」迪基慌里慌张地说，「可你就是*像*她。」

「你准是见过她的画像。国家肖像馆（National Portrait Gallery）里就挂着一幅。她娘家姓德拉梅尔（Delamere），我出嫁前也姓德拉梅尔。你瞧，亲爱的，她跟我原是一族的。」

===Original===
Dickie put his arms round her waist as she sat beside him, and laid his head on her shoulder.

“I wish you’d really been my mother,” he said, and his thoughts were back in the other days with the mother who wore a ruff and hoop. Lady Talbot hugged him tenderly.

“My dear little Dickie,” she said, “you don’t wish it as much as I do.”

“There are all sorts of things a chap can’t be sure of—things you mustn’t tell anyone. Secrets, you know—honorable secrets. But if it was your own mother it would be different. But if you haven’t got a mother you have to decide everything for yourself.”

“Won’t you let me help you?” she asked.

===Chinese===
迪基伸出两条胳膊，搂住坐在身旁的她，把头靠上她的肩。

「我真希望你原本就是我妈妈，」他说；他的心思已经回到了往日的那些时光，回到那位戴皱领、撑裙环的母亲身边。塔尔博特夫人温柔地搂紧了他。

「我亲爱的小迪基，」她说，「你盼得还没有我厉害呢。」

「有些事情，人是没法子拿准的——那种事你谁也不能告诉。秘密，你懂的——体面的秘密。可要是换了自己的亲妈，那就两样了。可要是一个人没有妈，他就什么都得自己拿主意。」

「你不肯让我帮你一把吗？」她问。

===Original===
Dickie, his head on her shoulder, was for one wild moment tempted to tell her everything—the whole story, from beginning to end. But he knew that she could not understand it—or even believe it. No grown-up person could. A chap’s own mother might have, perhaps—but perhaps not, too.

“I can’t tell you,” he said at last, “only I don’t think I want to be Lord Arden. At least, I do, frightfully. It’s so splendid, all the things the Ardens did—in history, you know. But I don’t want to turn people out—and you know Edred came and saved me from those people. It feels hateful when I think perhaps they’ll have to turn out just because I happened to turn up. Sometimes I feel as if I simply couldn’t bear it.”

“You dear child!” she said; “of course you feel that. But don’t let your mind dwell on it. Don’t think about it. You’re only a little boy. Be happy and jolly, and don’t worry about grown-up things. Leave grown-up things to the grownups.”

===Chinese===
迪基头靠着她的肩，有那么狂野的一瞬间，险些把一切都对她和盘托出——从头到尾的整个故事。可他知道，她不会懂，甚至不会信。哪个大人都不会信。自家亲妈兴许能信——可兴许也不能。

「我不能说给你听，」他末了说，「只是我想，我不想当阿登勋爵。至少，怕是又想得不得了。那多光彩呀，阿登家干下的那些大事——史书上都有，你是知道的。可我不愿意把人家赶出去——你知道，是埃德雷德跑来，把我从那些人手里救出来的。一想到兴许就因为我撞上门来，他们反倒得搬走，我心里就堵得慌。有时候我觉得，这我实在受不了。」

「你真是好孩子！」她说；「你自然会有这种感觉。可别让心思老拴在这上头。别去想它。你不过是个小孩子。快快活活的，别为大人的事操心。大人的事留给大人去操心吧。」

===Original===
“You see,” Dickie told her, “somehow I’ve always had to worry about grown-up things. What with Beale, and one thing and another.”

“That was the man you ran away from me to go to?”

“Yes,” said Dickie gravely; “you see, I was responsible for Beale.”

“And now? Don’t you feel responsible any more?”

“No,” said Dickie, in businesslike tones; “you see, I’ve settled Beale in life. You can’t be responsible for married people. They’re responsible for each other. So now I’ve got only my own affairs to think of. And the Ardens. I don’t know what to do.”

===Chinese===
「你瞧，」迪基对她说，「不知怎么，我总不得不为大人的事操心。一会儿是比尔，一会儿又这样那样。」

「就是那个你从我身边逃出去投奔的人？」

「是，」迪基郑重地说；「你瞧，我得对比尔负责任。」

「那么现在呢？你就不再负这个责任了？」

「不管了，」迪基办事人的口气说；「你瞧，比尔的生活我已安顿好了。结了婚的人，你是负不了责任的。他们彼此负责。所以如今我剩下的，只有自己的事要想了。还有阿登家的事。我不知道该怎么办。」

===Original===
“Do? why, there’s nothing *to* do except to enjoy yourself and learn your lessons and be happy,” she told him. “Don’t worry your little head. Just enjoy yourself, and forget that you ever had any responsibilities.”

“I’ll try,” he told her, and then the others came back with their peaches, and there was nothing more to be said but “Thank you very much” and goodbye.

---

Exploring the old smugglers’ caves was exciting and delightful, as exploring caves always is. It turned out that more than one old man in the village had heard from his father about the caves and the smuggling that had gone on in those parts in old ancient days. But they had not thought it their place to talk about such things, and I suspect that in their hearts they did not more than half believe them. Old Beale said—

“Why didn’t you ask me? I could a-told you where they was. Only I shouldn’t a done fear you’d break your precious necks.”

===Chinese===
「怎么办？嗨，没什么可*办*的呀，只消你玩得开心、功课上进、日子快活，」她对他说。「别叫你那个小脑袋瓜操心。你只管快活去，忘掉你从前担过的那些担子吧。」

「我试试看吧，」他应承她；随后那两个捧着桃子的回来了，剩下的也就只有「多谢多谢」和道别了。

---

探访那些老私贩子的洞窟，本就像探访一切洞窟一样，又刺激又快活。原来村里不止一位老爷爷，曾听自己的父亲讲起过这些洞窟，讲起老早老早的年代里那一带干过的私贩营生。只是他们觉得，这种事不该由自己张口去讲；我还疑心，他们心底里对那些话本来就半信半疑。老比尔说——

「你们怎么不问我？我早就能告诉你们洞在哪儿。只因怕你们摔断宝贝脖子，才一直没说。」

===Original===
Of course the children were desperately anxious to open up the brickwork and let the stream come out into the light of day; only their father thought it would be too expensive. But Edred and Elfrida worried and bothered in a perfectly gentle and polite way till at last a very jolly gentleman in spectacles, who came down to spend a couple of days, took their part. From the moment he owned himself an engineer Edred and Elfrida gave him no peace, and he seemed quite pleased to be taken to see the caves. He pointed out that the removal of the simple dam would send the water back into the old channel. It would be perfectly simple to have the brickwork knocked out, and to let the stream find its way back, if it could, to its old channel, and thence down the arched way which Edred and Elfrida told him they were certain was under a mound below the Castle.

“You know a lot about it, don’t you?” he said good-humoredly.

“Yes,” said Edred simply.

Then they all went down to the mound, and the engineer then poked and prodded it and said he should not wonder if they were not so far out. And then Beale and another man came with spades, and presently there was the arch, as good as ever, and they exclaimed and admired and went back to the caves.

===Chinese===
孩子们自然急得不得了，一心要拆开砖砌的障壁，放溪水重见天日；只是他们的父亲嫌花费太大。埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达便温温和和、客客气气地缠磨不休，到头来，一位下来小住两天的、戴眼镜的快活绅士站到了他们一边。打从他自认是工程师的那一刻起，埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达就没让他安生过；而他被领去看洞窟，似乎也颇为乐意。他指出，只要拆掉那道简陋的坝，水就会流回故道。把砖砌部分敲开是再简单不过的事，让溪水自己寻路回去——倘若回得成——回到故道，再顺着那条拱洞往下流；据埃德雷德和埃尔弗里达告诉他的，那拱洞准是在城堡下面一座土丘底下。

「你们对它知道得可不少，是不是？」他和颜悦色地说。

「是，」埃德雷德老老实实地说。

随后大家一齐下到土丘跟前，工程师这里戳戳、那里探探，说他们要真是弄错了，那倒叫人奇怪。接着比尔和另一个人扛着铁锹来了，不多时，那道拱券便现了形，完好如初；他们惊叹一番、赞叹一番，又回到洞窟去。

===Original===
It was a grand moment when the bricks had been taken out and daylight poured into the cave, and nothing remained but to break down the dam and let the water run out of the darkness into the sunshine. You can imagine with what mixed feelings the children wondered whether they would rather stay in the cave and see the dam demolished, or stay outside and see the stream rush out. In the end the boys stayed within, and it was only Elfrida and her father who saw the stream emerge. They sat on a hillock among the thin harebells and wild thyme and sweet lavender-colored gipsy roses, with their eyes fixed on the opening in the hillside, and waited and waited and waited for a very long time.

“Won’t you mind frightfully, daddy,” Elfrida asked during this long waiting, “if it turns out that you’re not Lord Arden?”

He paused a moment before he decided to answer her without reserve.

“Yes,” he said, “I shall mind, frightfully. And that’s just why we must do everything we possibly can to prove that Dickie is the rightful heir, so that whether he has the title or I have it you and I may never have to reproach ourselves for having left a single stone unturned to give him his rights—whatever they are.”

“And you, yours, daddy.”

“And me, mine. Anyhow, if he is Lord Arden I shall probably be appointed his guardian, and we shall all live together here just the same. Only I shall go back to being plain Arden.”

===Chinese===
砖头拆出来，日光灌进洞里，剩下的只是砸掉坝，放水走出黑暗、奔进阳光——那是个了不起的时刻。你可以想象，孩子们怀着怎样五味杂陈的心情：是宁可留在洞里看坝被拆毁呢，还是留在洞外看溪水冲出来？到头来两个男孩留在了洞里，亲眼见溪水出山的只有埃尔弗里达和她的父亲。父女俩坐在一座小丘上，四下里开着稀疏的圆叶风铃草、野百里香和甜甜的淡紫色野雏菊，眼睛一眨不眨地盯着山坡上的那个豁口，等呀，等呀，等了老长一段时间。

「爸爸，要是到头来你不是阿登勋爵，」在这漫长的等待里，埃尔弗里达问道，「你会非常非常难过吧？」

他顿了一顿，才决定毫不掩饰地回答她。

「是的，」他说，「我会难过，非常难过。可正因为这样，我们才必须竭尽一切可能去证明迪基是名正言顺的继承人。这样，无论名号归他还是归我，你我都可以不必责备自己——为着把他的权利还给他，我们不曾漏掉翻动一块石头——无论那权利是什么。」

「还有你，你的权利，爸爸。」

「还有我，我的。反正，他若是阿登勋爵，多半会指派我做他的监护人，我们仍旧一起住在这儿。只是我得退回去做一名无衔无位的阿登就是了。」

===Original===
“I believe Dickie *is* Lord Arden,” Elfrida began, and I am not at all sure that she would not have gone on to give her reasons, including the whole story which the Mouldiestwarp had told to Dickie; but at that moment there was a roaring, rushing sound from inside the cave, and a flash of shiny silver gleamed across that dark gap in the hillside. There was a burst of imprisoned splendor. The stream leaped out and flowed right and left over the dry grass, till it lapped in tiny waves against their hillock—“like sand castles,” as Elfrida observed. It spread out in a lake, wider and wider; but presently gathered itself together and began to creep down the hill, winding in and out among the hillocks in an ever-deepening stream.

“Come on, childie, let’s make for the moat. We shall get there first, if we run our hardest,” Elfrida’s father said. And he ran, with his little daughter’s hand in his.

They got there first. The stream, knowing its own mind better and better as it recognized its old road, reached the Castle, and by dinnertime all the grass round the Castle was under water. By teatime the water in the moat was a foot or more deep, and when they got up next morning the Castle was surrounded by a splendid moat fifty feet wide, and a stream ran from it, in a zigzag way it is true, but still it ran, to the lower arch under the mound, and disappeared there, to run underground into the sea. They enjoyed the moat for one whole day, and then the stream was dammed again and condemned to run underground till next spring, by which time the walls of the Castle would have been examined and concrete laid to their base, lest the water should creep through and sap the foundations.

===Chinese===
「我相信迪基*就是*阿登勋爵，」埃尔弗里达开了头；她究竟还会不会接着讲出理由——连莫迪耶斯特瓦普（Mouldiestwarp）讲给迪基听的那整个故事都算上——我说不准；可就在那一刻，洞里响起一阵轰隆奔涌的吼声，一道亮银的闪光掠过山坡上那黑黢黢的豁口。囚禁了许久的光华迸发了出来。溪水一跃而出，漫过干草，左一股右一股，直到细小的波浪拍上他们脚下的小丘——「像沙堆城堡似的」，埃尔弗里达说。水摊开成一面湖，越摊越宽；可是不多时便又聚拢起来，开始往山下爬，在小丘间曲一曲、绕一绕，越流越深。

「来，心肝儿，咱们往护城河那儿跑。拼足了劲儿跑，咱们准头一个到，」埃尔弗里达的父亲说。他牵着小女儿的手跑了起来。

果真是他们头一个到。溪水认出了自己的故道，越来越有主见，一路奔到城堡；到吃正饭的时候，城堡四周的草场全都漫在水里了。到喝茶的时候，护城河里的水深了一英尺还多；第二天早晨起身，一座五十英尺宽的堂皇护城河已经把城堡团团围住，还有一道溪水从河里流出去，弯弯曲曲是真的，可到底是在流——流到土丘下那一道较低的拱洞口，消失在那里，往地底下奔海去了。他们痛痛快快玩了护城河一整天；随后溪水又被拦住，判了下去年春天以前都在地底流刑；到那时候，城堡的墙身都将查验过，墙基要铺上混凝土，免得水渗过去，掏空了根基。

===Original===
“It’s going to be a very costly business, it seems,” Elfrida heard her father say to the engineer, “and I don’t know that I ought to do it. But I can’t resist the temptation. I shall have to economize in other directions, that’s all.”

When Elfrida had heard this she went to Dickie and Edred, who were fishing in the cave, and told them what she had heard.

“And we *must* have another try for the treasure,” she said. “Whoever has the Castle will want to restore it; they’ve got those pictures of it as it used to be. And then there are all the cottages to rebuild. Dear Dickie, you’re so clever, do think of some way to find the treasure.”

So Dickie thought.

And presently he said—

“You once saw the treasure being carried to the secret room—in a picture, didn’t you?”

They told him yes.

“Then why didn’t you go back to that time and see it really?”

“We hadn’t the clothes. Everything in our magic depended on clothes.”

“Mine doesn’t. Shall we go?”

===Chinese===
「瞧这样子，这可是件费大钱的工程，」埃尔弗里达听见父亲对工程师说，「我都不知道该不该办。可这个诱惑我抵挡不住。只好在别的方面省一省，就是这么回事。」

埃尔弗里达听见了这话，便去找正在洞里钓鱼的迪基和埃德雷德，把听见的话讲给他们。

「咱们*务必*再试一回找宝藏，」她说。「不管城堡归了谁，人家都要把它修复起来；他们手里有从前模样的那些图样呢。再说还有满村的村舍要重建。亲爱的迪基，你最聪明，替我们想个法子找着宝藏吧。」

于是迪基想。

不一会儿他说——

「你们从前见过宝藏运进密室——在一幅画里见过的，对不对？」

他们说是。

「那你们为什么不回到那个时代去，亲眼见一见呢？」

「我们没有那时的衣裳。我们的魔法，样样都指着衣裳。」

「我的魔法不指衣裳。咱们去不去？」
===Original===
“There were lots of soldiers in the picture,” said Edred, “and fighting.”

“I’m not afraid of soldiers,” said Elfrida very quickly, “and you’re not afraid of *anything*, Edred—you know you aren’t.”
